testing whether there is an age effect on the gene expression covariance matrices, i.e.
{\cal H}_{0}:Q^{*}(t)\equiv Q^{*}
for some
Q^{*}
.
The test we propose yields a p-value of 0.00019. When the analysis is performed separately for males and females, we obtain a p-value of 0.00034 for the male group and 0.00012 for the female group, suggesting a strong age effect on the gene expression covariance matrices.

Figure 6
:
Heatmap of the gene expression covariance matrices with diagonal elements omitted for individual at age
20,30,\ldots,90
, respectively.

7
Discussion

We have develop methods for statistical inference for the Fréchet regression on the Bures-Wasserstein manifold, where covariance matrix is treated as the outcome, including the uniform rate of convergence of the conditional Fréchet mean and the asymptotic distribution. Based on these results, we have further developed statistical test for testing the association between covariate outcome and Euclidean covariates. These results are further verified using simulations. We have demonstrated the methods by testing the association between gene co-expression and age, indicating the change of co-expressions among a set of genes in nutrient sensing pathway. The proposed methods have other applications, including in neuroimaging data analysis, where
covariance matrices (or correlation matrices after standardization) of multiple brain regions are used to summarize as functional connectivity matrices. The proposed methods can be used to identify the factors that are associated with such functional connectivity matrices.

In this paper, we assume that the outcome covariance matrices are observed and we only focus on the theoretical properties of the Fréchet regression estimator (
14
) under the complete observation setting. This is also the setting considered in
Petersen and Müller, (2019)
and
Petersen et al., (2021)
.
An important direction for future work is to develop the corresponding theoretical results for scenarios where the covariance matrices must be estimated from the data. Additionally, it would be valuable to relax the assumption on the eigenvalue lower bound and develop methods that can handle singular matrices.

Appendix A
Background on optimal transport and functional calculus

In this section, we collect relevant background about optimal transport and functional calculus to make the paper more self-contained.

A.1
Geometry of optimal transport

We begin with the geometry of optimal transport, and then specialize the general concepts to the Bures–Wasserstein manifold.
For introductory expositions of optimal transport, we refer to
Villani, (2003)
;
Santambrogio, (2015)
;
Panaretos and Zemel, (2020)
. For a more comprehensive treatment, we refer to
Ambrosio et al., (2005)
;
Villani, (2009)
.

Given
\mu_{0},\mu_{1}\in{\cal P}_{2,\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the constant-speed geodesic
\left(\mu_{t}\right)_{t\in[0,1]}
connecting
\mu_{0}
to
\mu_{1}
is characterized by

\mu_{t}=\left[\mathop{\mathrm{id}}+t\left(T_{\mu_{0}}^{\mu_{1}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\right)\right]_{\char 35\relax}\mu_{0},\qquad t\in[0,1]

Here,
\char 35\relax
denotes the pushforward operation defined by
T_{\char 35\relax}\mu(E)=\mu(T^{-1}(E))
for any Borel set
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then define the tangent vector of the geodesic
(\mu_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
at
t=0
to be the mapping
T_{\mu_{0}}^{\mu_{1}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}
. The tangent space
T_{\mu_{0}}{\cal P}_{2,\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
{\cal P}_{2,\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
at
\mu_{0}
is defined in
Ambrosio et al.,, 2005
, Thm 8.5.1
as

T_{\mu_{0}}{\cal P}_{2,\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):={\overline{\left\{\lambda(T_{\mu_{0}}^{\nu}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}):\lambda>0,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2,\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\right\}}}^{L^{2}(\mu_{0})}

Here the overline denotes closure with respect to the
L^{2}(\mu_{0})
measure.

Given two covariance matrices
Q,S\in{\cal S}^{++}_{d}
, the constant-speed geodesic connecting the corresponding centered Gaussians is given by

\left(I_{d}+t\left(T_{Q}^{S}-I_{d}\right)\right)Q\left(I_{d}+t\left(T_{Q}^{S}-I_{d}\right)\right),\quad t\in[0,1]

The tangent space
T_{Q}{\cal S}^{++}_{d}
can be identified with the space
{\cal S}_{d}
of symmetric
d\times d
matrices. For any
\widetilde{S}\in T_{Q}{\cal S}^{++}_{d}
, its norm in the tangent space is given by

\left\|\widetilde{S}\right\|_{Q}:=\left\langle\widetilde{S},Q\widetilde{S}\right\rangle^{1/2}

A.2
Functional calculus

To consider higher order differentials of
T^{S}_{Q}
on the Bures-Wasserstein manifold, we give a brief review of some key concepts in functional calculus that are essential for the development, and direct readers to
Dudley and Norvaiša, (2011)
for further details.

Let
Y
and
Z
be normed spaces with the norm on each denoted by
\left\|\cdot\right\|
, and let
U
be an open subset of
Y
. Let
L(Y,Z)
denote the space of all bounded linear operators from
Y
into
Z
. A function
\phi:U\to Z
is called (Fréchet) differentiable at
u\in U
if there exists an
L(u)\in L(Y,Z)
such that for each
y\in Y
with
u+Y\in Y
,

\lim_{y\to 0}\frac{\left\|\phi(u+y)-\phi(u)-L(u)y\right\|}{\left\|y\right\|}=0

The linear operator
L(u)
is unique, is called the derivative of
\phi
at
u
, and is denoted by
D\phi(u)
. If the function
\phi
is differentiable at each
u\in U
, then we say that
\phi
is differentiable on
U
.

To consider higher order derivatives of
\phi
, let
L^{1}(Y,Z):=L(Y,Z)
with the usual operator norm, and let
L^{k+1}(Y,Z):=L(Y,L^{k}(Y,Z))
with the operator norm recursively for
k=1,2,\ldots
. For
k\geq 2
, we say that
\phi
is (Fréchet) differentiable of order
k
at
u
if
\phi
has a
(k-1)
st derivative
D^{k-1}\phi(y)
at each point
y
of some neighborhood of
u
, and the mapping
D^{k-1}\phi
is differentiable at
u
. Then
D^{k}\phi(u)
, the
k
th derivative of
\phi
at
u
, is defined as the derivative of
D^{k-1}\phi
at
u
. If
\phi
is differentiable of order
k
at
u
for each
u\in U
then we say that
\phi
is differentiable of order
k
on
U
. A mapping
\phi
is called a
C^{k}
function on
U\subset Y
if the derivatives of
\phi
through order
k
all exist on
U
and are continuous. A mapping
\phi
is called a
C^{\infty}
function on
U\subset X
if it is a
C^{k}
function for each
k
.

It would be convenient to introduce the notion of multilinear mappings in order study the properties of higher order derivatives
D^{k}\phi
. A function
A:Y^{k}\to Z
is called
k
-linear if for each
j\in[k]
,
A(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})
is linear in
y_{j}
for any fixed values of
y_{i}
,
i\neq j
. The function
A
is called bounded if

\left\|A\right\|:=\sup\left\{\left\|A(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\right\|:\left\|y_{j}\right\|\leq 1,j\in[k]\right\}<\infty

(31)

Let
{\cal M}_{k}(Y,Z)
be the set of all bounded
k
-linear maps from
Y^{k}
to
Z
with norm define by (
31
).

The space
L^{k}(Y,Z)
can be identified with
{\cal M}_{k}(Y,Z)
through the natural isomorphism
\Phi_{(k)}:L^{k}(Y,Z)\to{\cal M}_{k}(Y,Z)
defined by

\displaystyle\Phi_{(k)}(A)(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})

\displaystyle:=A(y_{1})(y_{2})\cdots(y_{k})

\displaystyle:=\left[\cdots\left[A(y_{1})\right](y_{2})\cdots\right](y_{k})

Then the
k
th differential at
u
is defined by
d^{k}\phi(u):=\Phi_{(k)}(D^{k}\phi(u))
.

The
k
th differential
d^{k}\phi(u)
is symmetric in the sense that

d^{k}\phi(u)(y_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,y_{\sigma(k)})=d^{k}\phi(u)(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})

for any permutation
\sigma
of
[k]
. For simplicity, we also denote
d^{k}\phi(u)(y,\ldots,y)
by
d^{k}\phi(u)\cdot y^{\otimes k}
.

Let
{\cal M}_{k,s}(Y,Z)
denote the subspace of all symmetric elements of
{\cal M}_{k}(Y,Z)
. Then aside from the operator norm inherited from
{\cal M}_{k}(Y,Z)
, one can define another norm
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
on
{\cal M}_{k,s}(Y,Z)
by

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}:=\sup\left\{\left\|P(y,y,\ldots,y)\right\|:\left\|y\right\|\leq 1\right\}

Properties of the high order differentials
d^{k}T_{Q}^{S}
are investigated in Appendix
B.1
.

Appendix B
Technical lemmas

In this section, we collect technical lemmas and relevant notations for the proof. First, properties of differentials of
T^{S}_{Q}
are collected in Appendix
B.1
. Then various concentration results are given in Appendix
B.2
. Finally, properties of
F(\cdot,\cdot)
and
Q^{*}(\cdot)
are summarized in Appendix
B.3
.

B.1
Differentials of optimal transport maps

Recall that the optimal transport map between two centered Gaussian distributions
{\cal N}(0,Q)
and
{\cal N}(0,S)
has the closed-form expression
T_{Q}^{S}=S^{1/2}(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})^{-1/2}S^{1/2}
. In all relevant analysis, we need to consider differentials of
T_{Q}^{S}
when viewed as a function of
Q
for fixed
S
, which we denote as
d^{k}T^{S}_{Q}
for
k\geq 1
.
It is shown in
Kroshnin et al., (2021)
that
dT^{S}_{Q}
can be defined as follows. For any
H\in{\cal S}_{d}
,

dT_{Q}^{S}(H):=-S^{1/2}U^{\top}\Lambda^{-1/2}\delta\Lambda^{-1/2}US^{1/2}

where
U^{\top}\Lambda U
is an eigenvalue decomposition of
S^{1/2}QS^{1/2}
with
UU^{\top}=U^{\top}U=I
and
\delta=\left(\delta_{ij}\right)_{i,j=1}^{d}
with

\delta_{ij}=\begin{cases}\frac{\Delta_{ij}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}+\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}}&i,j\leq\operatorname{rank}(S)\\
0&\text{ otherwise }\end{cases},\qquad\Delta=US^{1/2}HS^{1/2}U^{\top}

To consider higher order differentials of
T^{S}_{Q}
, we start with the map
Q\mapsto Q^{1/2}
in Lemma
19
, next move on to
Q\mapsto Q^{-1/2}
in Lemma
20
which then leads to
T_{Q}^{S}
in Lemma
21
. Connections between
T_{Q}^{S}
and
W^{2}(Q,S)
are also investigated in Lemma
21
.

Lemma 19
.

The square root functional
\phi:Q\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}\to\phi(Q)=Q^{1/2}\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}
is Fréchet differentiable at any order on
{\cal S}_{d}^{++}
. Moreover, for any
Q\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}
and any
n\geq 0
, we have the estimates

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{n+1}\phi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{d,n}\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-(n+1/2)}

Here
C_{d,n}=d^{n/2}\cdot n!\binom{2n}{n}\cdot 2^{-(2n+1)}
.

Proof.

See
Del Moral and Niclas, (2018)
Theorem 1.1.

∎

Lemma 20
.

The inverse square root functional
\varphi:Q\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}\mapsto\varphi(Q)=Q^{-1/2}\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}
is Fréchet differentiable at any order on
{\cal S}_{d}^{++}
. Moreover, for any
A\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++},H\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
, the following holds.

\displaystyle d\varphi(Q)\cdot H

\displaystyle=-Q^{-1/2}\left(d\phi(Q)\cdot H\right)Q^{-1/2}

\displaystyle d^{2}\varphi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=-Q^{-1/2}\left(d^{2}\phi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 2}\right)Q^{-1/2}+2Q^{-1/2}(d\phi(Q)\cdot H)Q^{-1/2}(d\phi(Q)\cdot H)Q^{-1/2}

\displaystyle d^{3}\varphi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 3}

\displaystyle=-Q^{-1/2}\left(d^{3}\phi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 3}\right)Q^{-1/2}+3Q^{-1/2}\left(d^{2}\phi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 2}\right)Q^{-1/2}\left(d\phi(Q)\cdot H\right)Q^{-1/2}

\displaystyle+3Q^{-1/2}\left(d\phi(Q)\cdot H\right)Q^{-1/2}\left(d^{2}\phi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 2}\right)Q^{-1/2}

\displaystyle-6Q^{-1/2}\left(d\phi(Q)\cdot H\right)Q^{-1/2}\left(d\phi(Q)\cdot H\right)Q^{-1/2}\left(d\phi(Q)\cdot H\right)Q^{-1/2}

with

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d\varphi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{d,0}\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-3/2}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}\varphi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq(C_{d,1}+2C_{d,0}^{2})\cdot\left(\lambda_{\min}(Q)\right)^{-5/2}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{3}\varphi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{d,2}+6C_{d,1}C_{d,0}+6C_{d,0}^{3}\right)\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-7/2}

where
C_{d,n}
is defined in Lemma
19
.

Proof.

By Lemma
19
we have for infinitesimal
H\in{\cal S}_{d}
that

\displaystyle\left(Q+H\right)^{1/2}=Q^{1/2}+\underbrace{d\phi(Q)\cdot H}_{Z_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}d^{2}\phi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 2}}_{Z_{2}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{6}d^{3}\phi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 3}}_{Z_{3}}+\cdots

Let
Z=Z_{1}+Z_{2}+Z_{3}+Z_{4}
, and we obtain for infinitesimal
H\in{\cal S}_{d}
that

\displaystyle(Q+H)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(Q^{1/4}(I_{d}+Q^{-1/4}ZQ^{-1/4})Q^{1/4}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=Q^{-1/4}(I_{d}+Q^{-1/4}ZQ^{-1/4})^{-1}Q^{-1/4}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}Q^{-1/4}\left(I_{d}-Q^{-1/4}ZQ^{-1/4}+Q^{-1/4}ZQ^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/4}-Q^{-1/4}ZQ^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/4}+\cdots\right)Q^{-1/4}

\displaystyle=Q^{-1/2}-Q^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/2}+Q^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/2}-Q^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/2}ZQ^{-1/2}+\cdots

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}Q^{-1/2}-\underbrace{Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}}_{\text{1st order in }H}-\underbrace{Q^{-1/2}Z_{2}Q^{-1/2}+Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}}_{\text{2nd order in }H}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-Q^{-1/2}Z_{3}Q^{-1/2}+Q^{-1/2}Z_{2}Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}+Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}Z_{2}Q^{-1/2}-Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}Z_{1}Q^{-1/2}}_{\text{3rd order in }H}

\displaystyle+\text{higher order terms in }H

Here (i) follows from the von Neumann series expansion, and (ii) is obtained by arranging terms according to their orders in
H
. Then (
20
) follows,
and we have the following estimate

\displaystyle\left\|d^{2}\varphi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|Q^{-1/2}\left(d^{2}\phi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 2}\right)Q^{-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+2\left\|Q^{-1/2}(d\phi(Q)\cdot H)Q^{-1/2}(d\phi(Q)\cdot H)Q^{-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-1}\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}\phi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-3/2}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d\phi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|^{2}\cdot\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(C_{d,1}+2C_{d,0}^{2})\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-5/2}\cdot\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

and similarly

\displaystyle\left\|d^{3}\varphi(Q)\cdot H^{\otimes 3}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-1}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{3}\varphi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{3}+6\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-3/2}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}\varphi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|d\varphi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+6\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-2}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d\varphi(Q)\right|\!\right|\!\right|^{3}\cdot\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{d,2}+6C_{d,1}C_{d,0}+6C_{d,0}^{3}\right)\lambda_{\min}(Q)^{-7/2}\cdot\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{3}

Here the last inequality follows by applying Lemma
19
. And we arrive at the desired result.

∎

Lemma 21
.

The following properties hold for the 2-Wasserstein distance
W(Q,S)
and the optimal transport map
T_{Q}^{S}
. For any
Q,Q_{1},Q_{2}\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}
,
S\in{\cal S}_{d}^{+}
and
X,Y\in{\cal S}_{d}
,

(i)

W^{2}(Q,S)
is upper bounded by

W^{2}(Q,S)\leq 2d\left(\lambda_{\max}(Q)+\lambda_{\max}(S)\right)

(ii)

W^{2}(Q,S)
is twice differentiable with

\displaystyle d_{Q}W^{2}(Q,S)(X)=\left\langle I-T_{Q}^{S},X\right\rangle

\displaystyle d_{Q}^{2}W^{2}(Q,S)(X,Y)=-\left\langle X,dT_{Q}^{S}(Y)\right\rangle

Moreover, the following quadratic approximation holds:

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\left(1+\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}\left\langle-dT_{Q_{0}}^{S}(Q_{1}-Q_{0}),Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle W^{2}(Q_{1},S)-W^{2}(Q_{0},S)+\left\langle T_{Q_{0}}^{S}-I,Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\left(1+\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}\left\langle-dT_{Q_{0}}^{S}(Q_{1}-Q_{0}),Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\rangle

with
Q^{\prime}:=Q_{0}^{-1/2}Q_{1}Q_{0}^{-1/2}
.

(iii)

dT_{Q}^{S}
is self-adjoint, negative semi-definite and enjoys the following two-sided bound.

\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\left(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2}\right)}{2}\left\|Q^{-1/2}XQ^{-1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq\left\langle-dT_{Q}^{S}(X),X\right\rangle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2}\right)}{2}\left\|Q^{-1/2}XQ^{-1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}

(iv)

W^{2}(Q_{0},Q_{1})
can be upper and lower bounded by the Frobenius norm as follows

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{1+\lambda_{\min}^{-1}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{1}\right)}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq W^{2}(Q_{0},Q_{1})\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{1+\lambda_{\max}^{-1}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(Q_{1}\right)}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(v)

\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{k}T^{S}_{Q}\right|\!\right|\!\right|
can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{S}_{Q}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\min}\left(Q\right)^{-2}}{2}\cdot\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2}\right)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{S}_{Q}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(S\right)^{3}(C_{d,1}+2C_{d,0}^{2})\cdot\left(\lambda_{\min}\left(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2}\right)\right)^{-5/2}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{3}T^{S}_{Q}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(S\right)^{4}\left(C_{d,2}+6C_{d,1}C_{d,0}+6C_{d,0}^{3}\right)\left(\lambda_{\min}\left(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2}\right)\right)^{-7/2}

Moreover, if
S,Q\in{\cal S}_{d}(M^{-1},M)
, then we have

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{S}_{Q}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}M^{3}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{S}_{Q}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq(C_{d,1}+2C_{d,0}^{2})M^{8}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{3}T^{S}_{Q}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{d,2}+6C_{d,1}C_{d,0}+6C_{d,0}^{3}\right)M^{11}

Proof.

Proof of
(i)
:

By the closed form expression for
W^{2}(Q,S)
, one has

\displaystyle W^{2}(Q,S)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\left[Q+S-2(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\left[Q+S+2(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq d\left(\lambda_{\max}(Q)+\lambda_{\max}(S)+2\lambda_{\max}(Q)^{1/2}\lambda_{\max}(S)^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d\left(\lambda_{\max}(Q)+\lambda_{\max}(S)\right)

Proof of
(ii)
,
(iii)
:

see
Kroshnin et al., (2021, Lemma A.2, A.3, A.4, A.6)
.

Proof of
(iv)
:

Set
S=Q_{0}
in (
(ii)
), one can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\left(1+\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}\left\langle-dT_{Q_{0}}^{Q_{0}}(Q_{1}-Q_{0}),Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq W^{2}(Q_{1},Q_{0})

(32)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\left(1+\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}\left\langle-dT_{Q_{0}}^{Q_{0}}(Q_{1}-Q_{0}),Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\rangle

where
Q^{\prime}=Q_{0}^{-1/2}Q_{1}Q_{0}^{-1/2}
. Next, apply
(iii)
to get that for any
X\in{\cal S}_{d}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{\min}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{2}\lambda_{\max}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)\left\|X\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\left\langle-dT^{Q_{0}}_{Q_{0}}(X),X\right\rangle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)\left\|X\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(33)

Combine (
32
) and (
33
) to get that

\displaystyle W^{2}(Q_{1},Q_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{\left(1+\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{1+\lambda_{\min}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{1+\lambda_{\max}^{-1}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(Q_{1}\right)}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

and similarly

\displaystyle W^{2}(Q_{1},Q_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda_{\min}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{\left(1+\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{1+\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q_{0}\right)}{1+\lambda_{\min}^{-1}\left(Q_{0}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(Q_{1}\right)}\cdot\left\|Q_{1}-Q_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Proof of
(v)
:

First, note that results for
dT^{S}_{Q}
follows directly from
(iii)
.

Next since
T_{Q}^{S}=S^{1/2}(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})^{-1/2}S^{1/2}
, applying Lemma
20
gives that for
H
small enough, one has

\displaystyle d^{2}T^{S}_{Q}\cdot H^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=S^{1/2}\left[d^{2}\varphi(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})\cdot(S^{1/2}HS^{1/2})^{\otimes 2}\right]S^{1/2}

\displaystyle d^{3}T^{S}_{Q}\cdot H^{\otimes 3}

\displaystyle=S^{1/2}\left[d^{3}\varphi(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})\cdot(S^{1/2}HS^{1/2})^{\otimes 3}\right]S^{1/2}

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T_{Q}^{S}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}(S)^{3}\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}\varphi(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}(S)^{3}(C_{d,1}+2C_{d,0}^{2})\cdot\left(\lambda_{\min}(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})\right)^{-5/2}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{3}T_{Q}^{S}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}(S)^{4}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{3}\varphi(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}(S)^{4}\left(C_{d,2}+6C_{d,1}C_{d,0}+6C_{d,0}^{3}\right)\lambda_{\min}(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})^{-7/2}

∎

Lemma 22
.

Let
Y
and
Z
be normed spaces with the norm on each denoted by
\left\|\cdot\right\|
. For any
y\in Y
,
P\in{\cal M}_{k,s}(Y,Z)
,
k\geq 2
, let
Py
denote a
(k-1)
-linear function defined by

\displaystyle Py(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k-1})=P(y,y_{1},\ldots,y_{k-1})

Then
Py\in{\cal M}_{k-1,s}(Y,Z)
and

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Py\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\left\|P\right\|\left\|y\right\|\leq\frac{k^{k}}{k!}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\left\|y\right\|

(34)

Proof.

Py\in{\cal M}_{k-1,s}(Y,Z)
can be proved by definition. To prove (
34
), note that for any
\left\|\widetilde{y}\right\|\leq 1
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|Py(\widetilde{y},\ldots,\widetilde{y})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|P\right\|\cdot\left\|y\right\|\cdot\left\|\widetilde{y}\right\|^{k-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|P\right\|\cdot\left\|y\right\|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{k^{k}}{k!}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\cdot\left\|y\right\|

Here (i) follows from
Dudley and Norvaiša, (2011, Theorem 5.7)
.

∎

B.2
Concentration inequalities and uniform convergence

First, let us introduce some additional notation. Given a random variable
X
we denote
\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
for
\alpha>0
as follows.

\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}:=\inf\left\{\eta>0:\mathbb{E}\psi_{\alpha}\left(\left|X/\eta\right|\right)\leq 1\right\},\quad\text{where }\psi_{\alpha}(x):=\exp\left(x^{\alpha}\right)-1\quad\text{for }x\geq 0

(35)

Note that by the definition and Markov’s inequality, if
\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}<\infty
, then

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|X\right|\geq t\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\alpha}}{\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}^{\alpha}}\right)

(36)

For
\alpha\geq 1
,
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
is a norm
(
Vershynin,, 2018
)
, and for
\alpha<1
, it is equivalent to a norm
(
Talagrand,, 1989
)
.

Lemma 23
.

Let
X
be a random variable and
\alpha\geq 1
. Then the following properties are equivalent; the parameters
K_{i}>0
appearing in these properties differ from each other by at most an absolute constant factor depending on
\alpha
.

1.

The
\psi_{\alpha}
-norm of
X
satisfies
\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq K_{1}
.

2.

The tails of
X
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X\right|\geq t\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-t^{\alpha}/K_{2}^{\alpha}\right)\quad\forall t\geq 0.

3.

The moments of
X
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|X\right\|_{p}\leq K_{3}p^{1/\alpha}

Proof.

See
Vershynin, (2018, Exercise 2.7.3)
.
∎

For a random vector
X\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, denote

\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}:=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\left\|u\right\|=1}\left\|u^{\top}X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}

Lemma 24
.

Let
C_{1},c_{1},\alpha,\beta,B>0
. Then

\displaystyle\min\left\{1,B^{\beta}\cdot C_{1}\exp\left(-c_{1}t^{\alpha}\right)\right\}\leq C_{1}\exp\left(-c_{1}\frac{t^{\alpha}}{1+\frac{\beta\log\left(1\vee B\right)}{\log\left(1\vee C_{1}\right)}}\right)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume
C_{1},B\geq 1
. Denote

\displaystyle g(t)

\displaystyle:=C_{1}\exp\left(-c_{1}t^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle f(t)

\displaystyle:=C_{1}\exp\left(-c_{1}\frac{t^{\alpha}}{1+\frac{\beta\log B}{\log C_{1}}}\right)

f,g:(0,+\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
satisfy the following properties

•

g,f
are both positive and decreasing for
t\geq 0
.

•

denote
t_{0}=\left[\frac{\beta\log B+\log C_{1}}{c_{1}}\right]^{1/\alpha}
, then

\displaystyle 1\wedge g(t_{0})=1\leq f(t_{0})

•

derivatives satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left[\log(g(t))\right]\leq\frac{d}{dt}\left[\log(f(t))\right],\quad\forall t\geq t_{0}

Therefore,

\displaystyle 1\wedge g(t)\leq f(t),\quad\forall t>0

∎

Lemma 25
.

Let
\alpha\geq 1
. Let
X,Q
be random elements in
\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and let
\Phi_{k}
be a random symmetric operator in
{\cal L}\left((\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})^{\times k};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\right)
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Then

I.

\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq\left\|\left\|X\right\|_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq c_{0}(2d+1)^{1/\alpha}\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
for any
\alpha\geq 1
where
c_{0}>0
is some absolute constant independent of
d
and
\alpha
.

II.

If
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is symmetric, then for any
\left\|v\right\|=1
,

\displaystyle\left\|v^{\top}Qv\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq\left\|\left\|Q\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq c_{1}\left(2d\cdot\frac{\log 3}{\log 2}+1\right)^{1/\alpha}\sup_{\left\|v\right\|=1}\left\|v^{\top}Qv\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}

where
c_{1}>0
is some absolute constant independent of
d
and
\alpha
.

III.

For any
U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with unit
Frobenius
norm

\left\|\left\langle U,Q\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq\left\|\left\|Q\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\lesssim\sup_{U:\left\|U\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1}\left\|\left\langle U,Q\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}

IV.

For any
U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with unit Frobenius norm

\left\|\left\|\Phi_{k}\cdot U^{\otimes k}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|\Phi_{k}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\lesssim\sup_{U:\left\|U\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1}\left\|\left\|\Phi_{k}\cdot U^{\otimes k}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}

The constants behind
\lesssim
only depend on dimension
d
,
k
and possible on
\alpha
.

Proof.

I:
The first inequality
\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq\left\|\left\|X\right\|_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
follows since
\left\langle v,X\right\rangle\leq\left\|X\right\|
for any
\left\|v\right\|=1
.

For the second inequality, by Lemma
23
, we can assume without loss of generality that

\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
satisfies
K_{2}=1
, i.e.

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\|v\right\|=1}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\left\langle v,X\right\rangle\right|\geq t\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-t^{\alpha}\right)

Following the
1/2
-net argument in Lemma 1 in
Jin et al., (2019)
, one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|X\right\|\geq t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 4^{d}\sup_{\left\|v\right\|=1}\mathbb{P}(\left\langle v,X\right\rangle\geq t/2)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{2\cdot 4^{d}\exp\left(-t^{\alpha}/2^{\alpha}\right)}_{=:g(t)}

Lemma
24
implies that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|X\right\|\geq t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\alpha}}{(2d+1)\cdot 2^{\alpha}}\right)=:f(t)

Therefore, Lemma
23
implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\|X\right\|_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq c_{0}(2d+1)^{1/\alpha}\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}

where
c_{0}>0
is some absolute constant independent of
d
and
\alpha
.

II:
We prove the second inequality here. By Lemma
23
, we can assume without loss of generality that
\sup_{\left\|v\right\|=1}\left\|v^{\top}Qv\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
satisfies
K_{2}=1
, i.e.

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\|v\right\|=1}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|v^{\top}Qv\right|\geq t\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-t^{\alpha}\right)

Following the
1/4
-net argument in
Vershynin, (2018, Exercise 4.4.3, Theorem 4.4.5.)
, one can obtain that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\left\{\left\|\left\|Q\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\geq t\right\}\leq 9^{d}\cdot 2\exp(-t^{\alpha}/2^{\alpha})

Lemma
24
then implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\left\{\left\|\left\|Q\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\geq t\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\alpha}}{(2d\cdot\frac{\log 3}{\log 2}+1)\cdot 2^{\alpha}}\right),\quad\forall t\geq 0

Hence Lemma
23
implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\|Q\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq c_{1}\left(2d\cdot\frac{\log 3}{\log 2}+1\right)^{1/\alpha}\sup_{\left\|v\right\|=1}\left\|v^{\top}Qv\right\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}

where
c_{1}>0
is some absolute constant independent of
d
and
\alpha
.

The proofs for statements
III
and
IV
follow a similar approach.
∎

Next, we give some properties of the weights
w(x,X)
and
w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})
that will be crucial to prove uniform concentration as well as a central limit theorem for the estimate
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
. Recall that by definition,
w(x,X)=1+(x-\mu)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}(X-\mu)
and
w_{n,\rho}(x)=1+(x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
. For any vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, denote
\vec{z}=(1,z^{\top})^{\top}
and

\displaystyle\vec{\Sigma}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\vec{X}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\mu^{\top}\\
\mu&\Sigma+\mu\mu^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\vec{\Sigma}_{\rho}

\displaystyle:=\begin{pmatrix}1&\mu^{\top}\\
\mu&\Sigma_{\rho}+\mu\mu^{\top}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{where }\Sigma_{\rho}=\Sigma+\rho I_{p}

\displaystyle\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{X}_{i}\vec{X}_{i}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\widehat{\mu}^{\top}\\
\widehat{\mu}&\widehat{\Sigma}+\widehat{\mu}\widehat{\mu}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}

\displaystyle:=\begin{pmatrix}1&\widehat{\mu}^{\top}\\
\widehat{\mu}&\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}+\widehat{\mu}\widehat{\mu}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

Lemma 26
.

Suppose
X,X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathbb{P}\in{\cal P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
. Let
\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{X}_{i}\vec{X}_{i}^{\top}
. Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\displaystyle w(x,X)

\displaystyle=\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\vec{X}

(37)

\displaystyle w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})

\displaystyle=\vec{x}^{\top}\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1}\vec{X}

(38)

Proof.

For (
37
), by definition one has

\displaystyle\vec{\Sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\mu^{\top}\\
\mu&\Sigma+\mu\mu^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

Computing the inverse of the block matrix
\vec{\Sigma}
then gives

\displaystyle\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1+\mu^{\top}\Sigma^{-}\mu&-\mu^{\top}\Sigma^{-}\\
-\Sigma^{-}\mu&\Sigma^{-}\end{pmatrix}

Finally we arrive at (
37
) by computing
\vec{x}^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}\vec{X}
. (
38
) follows from similar arguments.
∎

Denote
L_{\tau}=C_{\psi_{2}}\sqrt{(1+\tau)\log n}
.

Lemma 27
.

Suppose Assumption
1
holds. Set

\displaystyle W_{0n}

\displaystyle:=W_{0n}(x)=-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)+\mu^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(x-\mu)

\displaystyle W_{1n}

\displaystyle:=W_{1n}(x)=\Sigma^{-1}(x-\mu)-\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)

one has

w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i})=W_{0n}+W_{1n}^{\top}X_{i}

(39)

Moreover, for any
\rho\in\left\{0,n^{-1}\right\}
,
L\geq 1
and
\tau\geq 0
, there exists constant
C>0
independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|>\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}C(1+\tau)\log n\right\}\lesssim n^{-(1+\tau)}

(40)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|>\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}C(1+\tau)\log n\right\}\lesssim n^{-(1+\tau)}

(41)

Proof.

Under Assumption
1
, we can assume without loss of generality that
\mu=0
and
\Sigma=I_{p}
. Then

\displaystyle w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})

\displaystyle=1+(x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)

\displaystyle w(x,X)

\displaystyle=1+x^{\top}X_{i}

Proof of (
39
):
Direct computation gives

\displaystyle w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i})

\displaystyle=(x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-x^{\top}X_{i}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{-x^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{=W_{0n}(x)}+\left(\underbrace{(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}-I_{p})x+\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{=W_{1n}(x)}\right)^{\top}X_{i}

Proof of (
40
), (
41
):

For
W_{0n}(x)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|>t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\right\}\cap\left\{L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq t/2\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{2<\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\right\}\cap\left\{L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq t/n\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq t/2\right\}\right)}_{\mathbf{(I)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{2<\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|\leq n\right\}\right)\wedge\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq t/n\right\}\right)}_{\mathbf{(II)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|>n\right\}}_{\mathbf{(III)}}

Note that for any
s\geq 0
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\frac{\sqrt{n}L}{\sqrt{n}L+1}s\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}L+1}s\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{n}L+1}s\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}L+1}s\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-cns^{2}/L^{2})+\exp(-c\sqrt{n}s/L)

•

If
\rho=0
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{(II)}+\mathbf{(III)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1/2\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-cn)

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|>t\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-cnt^{2}/L^{2})+\exp(-c\sqrt{n}t/L)+\exp(-cn)

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\exp(-c\sqrt{n}t/L)+\exp(-cn),&t\geq L/\sqrt{n}\\
\exp(-cnt^{2}/L^{2})+\exp(-cn),&t<L/\sqrt{n}\end{cases}

or equivalently for any
s>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|>\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\exp(-cs^{2})&s<1\\
\exp(-cs),&1\leq s\leq n\\
\exp(-cn),&s>n\end{cases}

•

If
\rho=1/n
, then
\mathbf{III}=0
. As a result, for any
s\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|>s\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-cs)+\exp(-cn)\wedge\begin{cases}\exp(-cs^{2}/n^{2}),&1\leq s\leq n\\
\exp(-cs/n),&s>n\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\exp(-cs)+\begin{cases}\exp(-cn),&1\leq s\leq n^{2}\\
\exp(-cs/n),&s>n^{2}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\exp(-cs),&1\leq s\leq n\\
\exp(-cn),&n<s\leq n^{2}\\
\exp(-cs/n),&s>n^{2}\end{cases}

For
W_{1n}(x)
:
note that
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}-I_{p}=-\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right)
, then one has

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|>t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}L+\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}L\geq\frac{L}{L+1}t\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\frac{1}{L+1}t\right\}

Hence for
L\geq 1
, one has

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|>t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\frac{t}{2L}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\frac{t}{2L}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\right\}\cap\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq t/(4L)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{2<\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\right\}\cap\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq t/(2nL)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\right\}\cap\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq t/(4L)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{2<\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\right\}\cap\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq t/(2nL)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\frac{t}{4L}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\frac{t}{4L}\right\}}_{\mathbf{(i)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{2<\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\right\}\wedge\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq t/(2nL)\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq t/(2nL)\right\}\right]}_{\mathbf{(ii)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n\right\}\right)}_{\mathbf{(iii)}}

•

If
\rho=0
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{(ii)}+\mathbf{(iii)}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>2\right\}\lesssim\exp(-cn)

As a result, for any
L\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|>t\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-cnt/L)\vee\exp(-cnt^{2}/L^{2})+\exp(-cnt^{2}/L^{2})+\exp(-cn)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\exp(-cnt^{2}/L^{2}),&t<L\\
\exp(-cn),&t\geq L\end{cases}

Therefore, for any
s>0
,
L\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|>\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}\lesssim\begin{cases}\exp(-cs^{2}),&s<\sqrt{n}\\
\exp(-cn),&s>\sqrt{n}\end{cases}

•

If
\rho=n^{-1}
, then
\mathbf{(iii)}=0
. As a result, for any
t=sL/\sqrt{n}
and
L\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|>\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\exp(-cs^{2}),&0<s<\sqrt{n}\\
\exp(-cn),&\sqrt{n}\leq s<n^{3/2}\\
\exp(-cs/\sqrt{n}),&s\geq n^{3/2}\end{cases}

Combining results for
W_{0n}(x)
and
W_{1n}(x)
, one can obtain that for any
\rho\in\left\{0,n^{-1}\right\}
,
L\geq 1
,
\tau\geq 0
and
1\leq s\leq\sqrt{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|>\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}\vee\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|>\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}\lesssim\exp(-cs)

By taking
s=O\left((1+\tau)\log n\right)
, the proof is then complete.
∎

Lemma 28
.

Let
\left\{(X_{i},Q_{i})\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
be i.i.d. samples satisfying Assumption
1
-
2
. Let

({\cal V},\left\|\cdot\right\|)
be a normed vector space and
\psi:{\cal S}_{d}^{++}\times\Theta\to{\cal V}
is a mapping parametrized by
\theta\in\Theta
. Suppose there exists an event
{\cal E}_{0}
under which

•

\left\|X_{i}-\mu\right\|\leq L_{n}
for
i=1,...,n
.

•

\left\|\psi(Q_{i},\theta)\right\|\leq K_{n}
for
i=1,...,n
uniformly for
\theta\in\Theta
.

Denote

\displaystyle G(L,\Theta;n)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i})\right]\psi(Q_{i};\theta)\right\|

Then for any
\rho\in\left\{0,n^{-1}\right\}
,
L\geq 1
and any
\tau\geq 0
, there exists constant
C
indpendent of
n
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{G(L,\Theta;n)\geq CK_{n}(L_{n}+1)\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\tau)\log n\right\}\leq

\displaystyle O\left(n^{-(1+\tau)}\right)+\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{0}^{c})

Proof.

Given
s\geq 1
, define event
{\cal E}_{1}(s)

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{1}(s):=\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i})\right]\psi(Q_{i};\theta)\right\|\geq 2K_{n}(L_{n}+1)\cdot\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\cal E}_{1}(s)\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\cal E}_{1}(s)\cap{\cal E}_{0}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\cal E}_{0}^{c}\right)

By (
39
) in Lemma
27
, one can obtain for any
x
and
\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\psi(Q_{i};\theta)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w(x,X_{i})\psi(Q_{i};\theta)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|W_{0n}(x)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(Q_{i};\theta)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{1n}(x)^{\top}X_{i}\psi(Q_{i};\theta)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\psi(Q_{i};\theta)\right\|+\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|_{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}\left\|\psi(Q_{i};\theta)\right\|

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{1}(s)\cap{\cal E}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left[\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|\cdot K_{n}+\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|_{2}\cdot L_{n}K_{n}\right]\geq 2K_{n}(L_{n}+1)\cdot\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left[\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|+\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|_{2}\right]\geq 2\cdot\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|W_{0n}(x)\right|\geq\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|W_{1n}(x)\right\|_{2}\geq\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}s\right\}

Applying (
40
) and (
40
) in Lemma
27
gives the desired result.
∎

We will need uniform upper bounds for various quantities of the form

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}f(Z_{1}^{n};\theta)

where
Z_{1}^{n}
denotes
(Z_{1},...,Z_{n})
.
To this end, we decompose the above quantity as follows.

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}f(Z_{1}^{n};\theta)-\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}f(Z_{1}^{n};\theta)}_{\mathrm{perturbation}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}f(Z_{1}^{n};\theta)}_{\mathrm{expectation}}

The expectation term is bounded in Lemma
29
below with a chaining argument, while the perturbation term is shown to concentrate in Lemma
31
by exploiting its bounded difference property. The proof of Lemma
29
is deferred to Appendix
B.2.1
.

Lemma 29
.

Given a function class

{\cal F}(\Theta)=\left\{f(\cdot;\theta):\mathbb{R}^{p}\to\mathbb{R}:\theta\in\Theta\right\}

where
f
is continuous jointly in
(z,\theta)
and
(\Theta,d)
is a separable metric space with finite diameter
D:=\sup_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta}d(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\gtrsim 1
.
Suppose a random vector
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
satisfies the following inequality

\left\|f(Z;\theta_{1})-f(Z;\theta_{2})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\tau\left(d(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\right),\quad\forall\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta

(42)

for some increasing function
\tau:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
that satisfies
\tau(0)=0,\tau(+\infty)=+\infty
. Then

•

the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|f(Z;\theta)\right|\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}\left|f(Z;\theta)\right|

(43)

where
C>0
is a fixed absolute constant and
N(\epsilon;\Theta)
is the
\epsilon
-covering number of
\Theta
.

•

Specifically, if
\tau
has the form

\tau(\epsilon)=\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\epsilon\vee\epsilon^{\alpha_{0}}\right)

for some constant
\alpha_{0}>0
, then for any
D\gtrsim 1
, one has

\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt\lesssim\frac{KD^{1\vee\alpha_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

(44)

•

Specifically, if
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
are i.i.d. and
f:(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{k}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
is equal to

\displaystyle f(Z_{1}^{n};\theta)=\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(Z_{i};\theta)-\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(Z_{i};\theta)\right|\!\right|\!\right|,\quad Z_{1}^{n}:=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n})

where
\psi(z;\theta)\in{\cal M}_{k,s}(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{R}^{m})
is a symmetric
k
-linear operator for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\theta\in\Theta
. Then the following inequality

\left\|f(Z_{1}^{n};\theta)-f(Z_{1}^{n};\widetilde{\theta})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|\psi(Z_{1};\theta)-\psi(Z_{1};\widetilde{\theta})\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

(45)

holds.

Remark 30
.

(
45
) will be applied in three ways as follows.

•

m=1,k=1
:
\psi(z;\theta)\in\mathbb{R}
and
\left|\!\left|\!\left|\psi(z;\theta)\right|\!\right|\!\right|
reduces to the absolute value of
\psi(z;\theta)
. Examples include
\psi(X,Q;x,S)=w(x,X)W^{2}(Q,S)
in Lemma
38

•

m=d,k=1
:
\psi(z;\theta)\in{\cal L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{R}^{m})
can be viewed as a matrix. Moreover,
\left|\!\left|\!\left|\psi(z;\theta)\right|\!\right|\!\right|
reduces to the matrix operator norm. Examples include
\psi(X,Q;x)=w(x,X)(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}(x)}-I_{d})
in Lemma
40
.

•

m=d\times d,k=1
: this is a special case of the previous one by identifying
d\times d
matrices as a vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
. Examples include
\psi(X,Q;x)=-w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(x)}
in Lemma
40
.

•

m=d\times d,k=2
: Examples include
\psi(X,Q;x,S)=w(x,X)d^{2}T^{Q}_{S}
in Lemma
40
.

Lemma 31
.

Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{q}
be a class of functions indexed by
\theta\in\Theta
. Suppose
\psi
is uniformly bounded in the sense that there exists a finite constant
K
such that
\left\|\psi(x;\theta)\right\|_{2}\leq K
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\theta\in\Theta
. For any fixed
y_{0}\in{\cal W}
, define
f:(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{n}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{q}
as

f(x_{1}^{n}):=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(x_{i};\theta)-y_{0}\right\|_{2}

Then
f
satisfies the bounded difference property with parameter
2K/n
, i.e. for any
x_{1}^{n},\widetilde{x}_{1}^{n}\in(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{n}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(x_{i}\neq\widetilde{x}_{i})\leq 1
,

\left\|f(x_{1}^{n})-f(\widetilde{x}_{1}^{n})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2K}{n},

(46)

Moreover, suppose
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
are i.i.d. random element in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, then

\left\|f(X_{1}^{n})-\mathbb{E}f(X_{1}^{n})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\frac{2K}{\sqrt{n}}

(47)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume
x_{1}\neq\widetilde{x}_{1}
and
x_{j}=\widetilde{x}_{j}
for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Then one has

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(x_{i};\theta)-y_{0}\right\|_{2}-\sup_{\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(\widetilde{x}_{i};\widetilde{\theta})-y_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(x_{i};\theta)-y_{0}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(\widetilde{x}_{i};\theta)-y_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(x_{i};\theta)-y_{0}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(\widetilde{x}_{i};\theta)+y_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\psi(x_{1};\theta)-\psi(\widetilde{x}_{1};\theta)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2K}{n}

Taking supremum over
\theta
then gives

f(x_{1}^{n})-f(\widetilde{x}_{1}^{n})\leq\frac{2K}{n}

The other direction can be obtained with the role
x_{1}^{n}
and
\widetilde{x}_{1}^{n}
reversed.
Therefore we get (
46
). By Corollary 2.21 in
Wainwright, (2019)
, we get (
47
). The proof is then complete.

∎

Finally, even though Theorem
6
shows fast convergence
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\to Q^{*}(x)
, one needs
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\to Q^{*}(\mu)
when considering power in Theorem
18
, and this is stated in Lemma
32
below. A crucial observation here is that
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
and
Q^{*}(\mu)
are the empirical and population Fréchet mean respectively. The proof is built upon
Le Gouic et al., (2022)
and
Altschuler et al., (2021)
.

Lemma 32
.

Assume Assumption
1
and
2
hold. Then there exists an event
\widetilde{E}
with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
under which the following holds

W^{2}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),Q^{*}(\mu)\right)\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(48)

As a result, under
\widetilde{E}
, one has

\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(49)

Proof.

Note that
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
and
Q^{*}(\mu)
are equal to the empirical and population barycenter respectively. For simplicity, we write
Q^{*}
for
Q^{*}(\mu)
.

Proof of (
48
):

From (3.8)-(3.9) in
Le Gouic et al., (2022)
, the variance inequality in
Chewi et al., (2020, Theorem 6)
and results in
Altschuler et al., (2021)
, one can obtain the following

W^{2}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),Q^{*}\right)\leq C_{b}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{Q^{*}}

(50)

where
C_{b}>0
is a constant independent of
n
and
\left\|A\right\|_{Q^{*}(\mu)}:=\left\langle A,Q^{*}A\right\rangle
. Moreover, Lemma
33
implies that
c_{1}^{-1}I_{d}\preceq Q^{*}\preceq c_{1}I_{d}
; see Assumption
2
for the definition of
c_{1}
. As a result, one can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{Q^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{*}\right)\cdot\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\Lambda}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(51)

Note that by the optimality condition for barycenter, one has
\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)=0
. Then one can apply Lemma
25
to get that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\left\|U\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}\left\|\left\langle U,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\left\|U\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\left\langle U,T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\left\|T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

(52)

Recall that
T^{Q}_{S}=S^{-1/2}\left(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}S^{-1/2}
, then one has

\displaystyle\left\|\left\|T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\left\|\left\|T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|1+c_{\Lambda}^{3/2}\lambda_{\max}\left(Q\right)^{1/2}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\left\|1+c_{\Lambda}^{3/2}c_{\Lambda}^{1/2}\left\|X-\mu\right\|^{1/2}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|1+c_{\Lambda}^{3/2}c_{\Lambda}^{1/2}\left(1\vee\left\|X-\mu\right\|\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|1+c_{\Lambda}^{2}\left(1+\left\|X-\mu\right\|\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1

(53)

Here (i) follows since dimension
d
is fixed and absorbed into the constant factor independent of
n
, (ii) is a result of Assumption
2
.

Finally, combining (
50
) (
51
) (
52
) and (
53
) gives

\left\|W^{2}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),Q^{*}(\mu)\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}

(54)

which implies (
48
).

Proof of (
49
):

Apply (
(iv)
) in Lemma
21
to get that under the event
\widetilde{E}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq W^{2}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),Q^{*}\right)\cdot\frac{1+\lambda_{\min}^{-1}\left(Q^{*}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)}{\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{*}\right)\cdot\lambda_{\min}^{-2}\left(Q^{*}\right)}

(55)

\displaystyle\lesssim W^{2}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),Q^{*}\right)\cdot\left(1+\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\left(1+\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)\right)

for constant
C>0
large enough. Note that this implies

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{*}\right)+\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\Lambda}+C\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\left(1+\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)\right)

Solving for
\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)
gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\Lambda}+C\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}}{1-C\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}}

(56)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1\qquad\text{for }n\text{ large enough}

Finally, plugging (
56
) back into (
55
) gives (
49
).

∎

B.2.1
Proof of Lemma
29

Proof of (
43
):

Since
f(z,\theta)
is continuous in
\theta
and
\Theta
is separable, we have for any countable, dense subset
\mathbb{U}\subset\Theta
, the following equality

\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|f(Z,\theta)\right|=\mathbb{E}\sup_{S\in\mathbb{U}}\left|f(Z,\theta)\right|

holds. By the monotone convergence theorem, it suffices to assume that
\mathbb{U}
is finite and get an upper bound that is independent of the cardinality of
\mathbb{U}
.

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
\mathbb{U}_{k}\subset\mathbb{U}
be a minimal
\epsilon_{k}
-covering set of
\mathbb{U}
where
\epsilon_{k}
is defined by

\epsilon_{k}=\tau^{-1}(2^{-k})

(57)

Let
N(\epsilon_{k},\mathbb{U})
be the cardinality of the minimal
\epsilon_{k}
-covering set
\mathbb{U}_{k}
of
\mathbb{U}
. Since
\mathbb{U}
is a subset of
\Theta
,
N(\epsilon_{k},\mathbb{U})
can be upper bounded by

\log N(\epsilon_{k};\mathbb{U})=\log\left|\mathbb{U}_{k}\right|\leq\log N(\epsilon_{k};\Theta)

Since
\mathbb{U}
is finite, there is a largest
\eta\in\mathbb{Z}
and a smallest integer
H\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\mathbb{U}_{\eta}=\left\{\theta_{0}\right\}\text{ for some }\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{U},\qquad\mathbb{U}_{H}=\mathbb{U}

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, define the mapping
\pi_{k}:\Theta\to\mathbb{U}_{k}
via

\pi_{k}(\theta)=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\widetilde{\theta}\in\mathbb{U}_{k}}d(\widetilde{\theta},\theta)

so that
\pi_{k}(S)
is the best approximation of
\theta\in\Theta
from the set
\mathbb{U}_{k}
.

For any
\theta\in\mathbb{U}
, apply the triangle inequality to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}f(Z,\theta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}\left(f(Z,\theta)-f(Z,\theta_{0})\right)\right|+\mathbb{E}\left|f(Z;\theta_{0})\right|

(58)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}\left|f(Z,\theta)-f(Z,\theta_{0})\right|+\mathbb{E}\left|f(Z;\theta_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}\left|\sum_{k=\eta+1}^{H}\left(f(Z,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(Z,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right)\right|+\mathbb{E}\left|f(Z;\theta_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}\sum_{k=\eta+1}^{H}\left|f(Z,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(Z,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right|+\mathbb{E}\left|f(Z;\theta_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=\eta+1}^{H}\mathbb{E}\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}\left|f(Z,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(Z,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right|+\mathbb{E}\left|f(Z;\theta_{0})\right|

Here (i) is a consequence of decomposing
f(Z,\theta)-f(Z,\theta_{0})
as a telescoping sum

f(Z,\theta)-f(Z,\theta_{0})=\sum_{k=\eta+1}^{H}\left(f(Z,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(Z,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right)

For any fixed
\theta\in\Theta
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|f(\cdot,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(\cdot,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f(\cdot,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(\cdot,\theta)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}+\left\|f(\cdot,\theta)-f(\cdot,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(I)}{\leq}\tau(\epsilon_{k})+\tau(\epsilon_{k-1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\tau(\epsilon_{k-1})

\displaystyle\overset{(II)}{=}2\cdot 2^{-(k-1)}

Here (I) is a result of (
42
), and (II) follows from (
57
). Then, one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}\left|f(Z,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(Z,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\left|\mathbb{U}_{k-1}\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{U}_{k}\right|}\cdot 2\cdot 2^{-(k-1)}

(59)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon_{k};\Theta)}\cdot 2^{-(k-1)}

Here (i) follows from the properties of the maximum of finitely many sub-Gaussian random variables and the fact that the maximum is taken over at most
\left|\mathbb{U}_{k-1}\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{U}_{k}\right|\leq N(\epsilon_{k};\Theta)^{2}
random variables. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\eta+1}^{H}\mathbb{E}\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{U}}\left|f(Z,\pi_{k}(\theta))-f(Z,\pi_{k-1}(\theta))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=\eta+1}^{H}\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon_{k};\Theta)}\cdot 2^{-(k-1)}

(60)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=\eta+1}^{H}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(2^{-k});\Theta)}\cdot 2^{-(k-1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt

\displaystyle\overset{(I)}{=}\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt

Here (I) follows by noticing that
\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)=0
for any
t\geq\tau(D)
.

Combine (
58
) and (
60
) to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|f(Z;\theta)\right|\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}\left|f(Z;\theta)\right|

for some constant
C>0
.

Proof of (
44
):

Consider two cases
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1]
and
\alpha_{0}>1
.

Case I
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1]
:

Note that since
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1]
, one has

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}\right)\wedge\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}\right)^{1/\alpha_{0}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}&\sqrt{n}t\geq K\\
\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}\right)^{1/\alpha_{0}}&\sqrt{n}t\leq K\end{cases}

(61)

As a result, one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt

(62)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N\left(\tau^{-1}\left(\frac{t}{\sqrt{n}}\right);\Theta\right)}\cdot dt

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{K}\sqrt{\log N\left((t/K)^{1/\alpha_{0}};\Theta\right)}\cdot dt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{K}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N\left(t/K;\Theta\right)}\cdot dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{K}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{(t/K)^{1/\alpha_{0}}}\right)}\cdot dt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{K}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{(t/K)}\right)}\cdot dt

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{=}\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{t^{1/\alpha_{0}}}\right)}\cdot dt}_{a_{1}}+\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}\underbrace{\int_{1}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)/K}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{t}\right)}\cdot dt}_{a_{2}}

Here (i), (iii) follows from a change of variables, and (ii) follows from (
61
).

•

a_{1}
: one has

\displaystyle a_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{1}{t^{1/\alpha_{0}}}\right)}dt+\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}dt

(63)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

Here (i) follows from the inequality
\sqrt{s+t}\leq\sqrt{s}+\sqrt{t}
for
s,t\geq 0
, and (ii) follows from the assumption that
D\gtrsim 1
.

•

a_{2}
: for
D\gtrsim 1
,

\displaystyle a_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\int_{1}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)/K}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}dt

(64)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}{K}\cdot\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

\displaystyle=\left(D\vee D^{\alpha_{0}}\right)\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\lesssim}D\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

Here (i) a consequence of the fact that
D/t\leq D
for
t\geq 1
, (ii) results from substituting the definition of
\tau(\cdot)
and (iii) follows from the assumption
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1]
.

Combine (
62
), (
63
) and (
64
) to see that

\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt\lesssim\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}D\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

which proves (
44
) for
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1]
.

Case II
\alpha_{0}>1
:

for
\alpha_{0}>1
, one has

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}\right)\wedge\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}\right)^{1/\alpha_{0}}

(65)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}&\sqrt{n}t\leq K\\
\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}t}{K}\right)^{1/\alpha_{0}}&\sqrt{n}t>K\end{cases}

As a result, one can obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt

(66)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N\left(\tau^{-1}\left(\frac{t}{\sqrt{n}}\right);\Theta\right)}\cdot dt

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{K}\sqrt{\log N\left(t/K;\Theta\right)}\cdot dt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{K}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N\left((t/K)^{1/\alpha_{0}};\Theta\right)}\cdot dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{K}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{t/K}\right)}\cdot dt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{K}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{(t/K)^{1/\alpha_{0}}}\right)}\cdot dt

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{=}\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{t}\right)}\cdot dt}_{a_{3}}+\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}\underbrace{\int_{1}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)/K}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{D}{t^{1/\alpha_{0}}}\right)}\cdot dt}_{a_{4}}

Here (i), (iii) follows from a change of variables, and (ii) follows from (
65
).

•

a_{3}
: one has

\displaystyle a_{3}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log^{+}\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)}dt+\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}dt

(67)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

Here (i) follows from the inequality
\sqrt{s+t}\leq\sqrt{s}+\sqrt{t}
for
s,t\geq 0
, and (ii) follows from the assumption that
D\gtrsim 1
.

•

a_{4}
: for
D\gtrsim 1
,

\displaystyle a_{4}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\int_{1}^{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)/K}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}dt

(68)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{\sqrt{n}\tau(D)}{K}\cdot\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

\displaystyle=\left(D\vee D^{\alpha_{0}}\right)\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\lesssim}D^{\alpha_{0}}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

Here (i) a consequence of the fact that
D/\left(t^{1/\alpha_{0}}\right)\leq D
for
t\geq 1
, (ii) results from substituting the definition of
\tau(\cdot)
and (iii) follows from the assumption
\alpha_{0}>1
.

Combine (
66
), (
67
) and (
68
) to see that

\int_{0}^{\tau(D)}\sqrt{\log N(\tau^{-1}(t);\Theta)}\cdot dt\lesssim\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}D^{\alpha_{0}}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}

which proves (
44
) for
\alpha_{0}>1
.

Proof of (
45
):

Let
\Psi_{n}(\theta):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(Z_{i};\theta)
, then one has

\displaystyle\left\|f(Z_{1}^{n};\theta)-f(Z_{1}^{n};\widetilde{\theta})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|\Psi_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\Psi_{n}(\theta)\right|\!\right|\!\right|-\left|\!\left|\!\left|\Psi_{n}(\widetilde{\theta})-\mathbb{E}\Psi_{n}(\widetilde{\theta})\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|\Psi_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\Psi_{n}(\theta)-\Psi_{n}(\widetilde{\theta})+\mathbb{E}\Psi_{n}(\widetilde{\theta})\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\sup_{u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\left\|\left\langle u,\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(\theta)\right]-\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta})+\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta})\right]\cdot v^{\otimes k}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\left\|\left\langle u,\left[\psi(Z;\theta)-\psi(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(Z;\theta)-\psi(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right]\cdot v^{\otimes k}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\lesssim}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\left\|\left\langle u,\left[\psi(Z;\theta)-\psi(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right]\cdot v^{\otimes k}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\left\|\left\|\left[\psi(Z;\theta)-\psi(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right]\cdot v^{\otimes k}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\leq}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|\psi(Z;\theta)-\psi(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|v\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{k}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|\psi(Z;\theta)-\psi(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

Here (i) is a result of Lemma
25
, (ii) arises due to independence, (iii) follows from Lemma 2.6.8 in
Vershynin, (2018)
, and (iv) is derived from the definition of
\left|\!\left|\!\left|\cdot\right|\!\right|\!\right|
. The proof is then complete.

B.3
Properties of
F
and
Q^{*}

This section collects properties of
F(\cdot,\cdot)
and
Q^{*}(\cdot)
that are needed later in the proof.

Lemma 33
.

Assume Assumption
1
and
2
holds. Then the following inequalities

\displaystyle\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|x-\mu\right\|)^{-1}I_{d}\preceq

\displaystyle Q^{*}(x)\preceq\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|x-\mu\right\|)I_{d},\qquad\forall x\in\supp X

(69)

Moreover, there exist constant
C_{0}^{*},C_{1}^{*}>0
which only depend on
c_{\Sigma},C_{\psi_{2}}
from Assumption
1
and
c_{\Lambda},C_{1}
(from Assumption
2
) such that

\displaystyle Q^{*}(x)\preceq\left[C_{0}^{*}+C_{1}^{*}\left\|x-\mu\right\|\right]^{2}I_{d}

(70)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume
X
have expectation
\mu=0
and covariance
\Sigma=I_{p}
. Denote
\lambda_{1}(x)=\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{*}(x)\right)
and
\lambda_{d}(x)=\lambda_{\min}\left(Q^{*}(x)\right)

Proof of (
69
):

By the optimality condition of
Q^{*}(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{S}\mathbb{E}\left[W^{2}(S,Q)\big|X=x\right]
. By the differential properties of
W^{2}
in Lemma
21
, one can obtain

\displaystyle I_{d}=\mathbb{E}\left[T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}\big|X=x\right]

Recall that
T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}={Q^{*}(x)}^{-1/2}\left(Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}QQ^{*}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}Q^{*}(x)^{-1/2}
, multiplying both sides of the equation with
Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}
then gives

Q^{*}(x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}QQ^{*}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\big|X=x\right]

Then one can obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}QQ^{*}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\mid X=x\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda_{\max}\left(\left(Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}QQ^{*}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\right)\mid X=x\right]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|x-\mu\right\|)^{1/2}\lambda_{1}(x)^{1/2}

Here the second line follows from the convexity of the largest eigenvalue function
\lambda_{\max}\left(\cdot\right)
over PSD matrices. The last inequality follows from Assumption
2
. Therefore, one can readily obtain
\lambda_{1}(x)\leq\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|x-\mu\right\|)
. The lower bound
\lambda_{d}(x)\geq\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|x-\mu\right\|)^{-1}
can be derived similarly by the concavity of the function
\lambda_{\min}\left(\cdot\right)
.

Proof of (
70
):

By the optimality condition of

Q^{*}(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{S}\mathbb{E}w(x,X)W^{2}(S,Q)
, one has

\displaystyle 0=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

Note that
\mathbb{E}w(x,X)=1
for any
x
, hence one has

\displaystyle I_{d}=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}

Recall that
T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}={Q^{*}(x)}^{-1/2}\left(Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}QQ^{*}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}Q^{*}(x)^{-1/2}
, multiplying both sides of the equation with
Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}
then gives

\displaystyle Q^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}Q_{i}Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}

Taking the largest eigenvalue on both sides, one has

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|w(x,X)\right|\cdot\left\|\left(Q^{*}(x)^{1/2}QQ^{*}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|w(x,X)\right|\cdot\lambda_{\max}(Q)^{1/2}\lambda_{1}^{1/2}(x)

which implies

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mathbb{E}\left(1+\left\|x\right\|\cdot\left\|X\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q)^{1/2}\right]^{2}

Under Assumption
1
and Assumption
2
, we have
\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})\leq\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|)
, which implies that

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mathbb{E}\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|X\right\|)^{1/2}+\left\|x\right\|\mathbb{E}\left\|X\right\|\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|X\right\|)^{1/2}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[C_{0}^{*}+C_{1}^{*}\left\|x\right\|\right]^{2}

where
C_{0}^{*},C_{1}^{*}>0
are finite constants only depend on
c_{\Sigma},C_{\psi_{2}}
from Assumption
1
and
c_{\Lambda},C_{\Lambda}
(from Assumption
2
).
∎

Lemma 34
.

Assume Assumption
1
and
2
hold. If
X
and
Q
are independent, then the following holds.

•

Q^{*}(x)\equiv Q^{*}(\mu)

•

Assumption
5
holds for
C_{\lambda}=0
and
c_{\lambda}
large enough.

•

Assumption
4
holds for
\delta_{F}=1
,
\alpha_{F}=2
,
C_{F}=1
and some constant
c_{F}
large enough.

Proof.

Independence between
X
and
Q
implies that

\displaystyle F(x,S)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)W^{2}(S,Q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\cdot\mathbb{E}W^{2}(S,Q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}W^{2}(S,Q)

The existence and uniqueness of the minimizer of
F(x,\cdot)
follow from
Panaretos and Zemel, (2020, Proposition 3.2.3, Proposition 3.2.7)
. Moreover, since
\mathbb{E}W^{2}(S,Q)
does not depend on
x
, we have
Q^{*}(x)\equiv Q^{*}(\mu)
. We denote
Q^{*}:=Q^{*}(\mu)
from now on. Note that Lemma
33
implies that
Q^{*}\in{\cal S}_{d}(c_{\Lambda}^{-1},c_{\Lambda})

Verification of Assumption
5
:

Again by independence, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[-w(x,X)dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[-dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q}\right]

Therefore, one can obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[-w(x,X)dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[-dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\geq}\mathbb{E}\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}(Q^{1/2}Q^{*}Q^{1/2})}{2}\cdot\lambda_{\min}^{2}(Q^{*-1})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbb{E}\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}(Q)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\gtrsim}\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|X-\mu\right\|)\right]^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{1\vee\left\|X-\mu\right\|^{C_{\Lambda}/2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\geq}c_{\mathrm{eig}}

for some constant
c_{\mathrm{eig}}
small enough. Here (i) follows form Lemma
21
, (ii) is a result of Assumption
2
and (iii) is due to Assumption
1
. The proof is then complete.

Verification of Assumption
4
:

In order to verify (
12
). Let us denote
Q^{\prime}=Q^{*-1/2}SQ^{*-1/2}
. Then (
(ii)
) and (
(iii)
) in Lemma
21
imply the following lower bound.

\displaystyle F(x,S)-F(x,Q^{*})

(71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[W^{2}(S,Q)-W^{2}(Q^{*},Q)\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\langle I_{d}-T^{Q}_{Q^{*}},S-Q^{*}\right\rangle+\mathbb{E}\frac{2}{\left(1+\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}(Q^{\prime}(x))\right)^{2}}\left\langle-dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q}(S-Q^{*}),S-Q^{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\frac{2}{\left(1+\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}(Q^{\prime}(x))\right)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\left(Q^{1/2}Q^{*}Q^{1/2}\right)}{2}\cdot\left\|Q^{*-1/2}(S-Q^{*})Q^{*-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Here (i) follows from the fact that
\mathbb{E}(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d})=0
which is a consequence of the optimality condition and independence.

Triangle inequality implies that

\displaystyle\left\{S\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}:\delta\leq\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Delta\right\}\subset\left\{S\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}:\left\|S\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq c_{\Lambda}+\Delta\right\}

which combined with (
71
) implies for any
\Delta\geq\delta
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\delta\leq\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Delta}F(x,S)-F(x,Q^{*})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{\delta\leq\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Delta}\mathbb{E}\frac{2}{\left(1+\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}(Q^{\prime})\right)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\left(Q^{1/2}Q^{*}Q^{1/2}\right)}{2}\cdot\left\|Q^{*-1/2}(S-Q^{*})Q^{*-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\gtrsim}

\displaystyle\inf_{\delta\leq\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Delta}\frac{1}{\left(1+\Delta^{1/2}\right)^{2}}\cdot\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\gtrsim}

\displaystyle\inf_{\delta\leq\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Delta}\frac{1}{1+\Delta}\cdot\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{sep}}\frac{1}{\Delta}\delta^{2}

where
c_{\mathrm{sep}}>0
is a constant independent of
n
that is small enough. Here (i) follows from the inequalities that
\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\lesssim\lambda_{\max}\left(S\right)\lesssim\Delta
,
\lambda_{\min}\left(Q^{1/2}Q^{*}Q^{1/2}\right)\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}\left(Q\right)
and
\mathbb{E}\lambda_{\min}\left(Q\right)^{1/2}\gtrsim 1
; (ii) holds by choosing
\delta_{F}=1
so that
\Delta\geq\delta_{F}=1
.

Combining results above, Assumption
4
holds with with
\delta_{F}=1
,
\alpha_{F}=2
,
C_{F}=1
and
c_{F}=1/c_{\mathrm{sep}}
. The proof is then complete.

∎

Recall the definition of
\gamma_{\Lambda}(\cdot)
from Assumption
2
and the definition of
\alpha_{F},\delta_{F},C_{F}
from Assumption
4
. Denote
M_{L}:=\gamma_{\Lambda}(L)
for any
L>0
. With these notations in place, we now present the continuity theorem for
Q^{*}(\cdot)
as follows.

Lemma 35

(Hölder continuity of
Q^{*}(\cdot)
)
.

Suppose Assumption
1
-
5
hold. Then for any
L\geq e
that satisfies
M_{L}>\delta_{F}
, any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
,

\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|\leq C_{H}\max\left\{\left(L^{C_{F}}M_{L}^{C_{F}+1}\right)^{1/\alpha_{F}}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|^{1/\alpha_{F}},L^{C_{F}}M_{L}^{C_{F}+1}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\right\}

(72)

holds as long as the constant
C_{H}
is large enough. Here
C_{H}
is independent of
n
but depends on
d
.

Proof.

Note that
M_{L}\geq 1
by the properties of
\gamma_{\Lambda}
in Assumption
2
. The proof is divided into 3 steps.

1.

First, we should that
F(x,S)
is Lipschitz in
x
for any fixed
S\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{L}^{-1},M_{L})
.

2.

Next, the Lipschitzness in step 1 and Assumption
4
imply the local Hölder continuity of
Q^{*}(x)
with respect to
x
.

3.

Then, an argument based on linear interpolation between
x
and
\widetilde{x}
implies Lipschitz continuity for
x,\widetilde{x}
separated far apart.

Combining the 3 step above finally gives (
72
).

To start with, note that Lemma
33
implies

Q^{*}(x)\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{L}^{-1},M_{L}),\quad\text{for any }x\in B(\mu,L)

Recall that
F(x,S)=\mathbb{E}\left[w(x,X)W^{2}(S,Q)\right]
.

1.
F
is Lipschitz in
x
:

for any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
, one can obtain

\displaystyle\left|F(x,S)-F(\widetilde{x},S)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}(x-\widetilde{x})\Sigma^{-1}(X-\mu)W^{2}(S,Q)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\|(X-\mu)W^{2}(S,Q)\right\|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\|X-\mu\right\|\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(S\right)+\lambda_{\max}\left(Q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\cdot\left[\mathbb{E}\left\|X-\mu\right\|\lambda_{\max}\left(S\right)+\mathbb{E}\left\|X-\mu\right\|\lambda_{\max}\left(Q\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(S\right)+1\right)

(73)

Here (i) follows from (
(i)
) in Lemma
21
, and (ii) follows from the concentration of
X
(Assumption
1
) and boundedness of
Q
given
X
(Assumption
2
).

2. Local Hölder continuity:

for any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
, one has

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle F(x,Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))-F(x,Q^{*}(x))

(74)

\displaystyle=
