\displaystyle\left(F(x,Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))-F(\widetilde{x},Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))\right)+\underbrace{\left(F(\widetilde{x},Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))-F(\widetilde{x},Q^{*}(x))\right)}_{\leq 0}+\left(F\left(\widetilde{x},Q^{*}(x)\right)-F(x,Q^{*}(x))\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|F(x,Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))-F(\widetilde{x},Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))\right|+\left|F\left(\widetilde{x},Q^{*}(x)\right)-F(x,Q^{*}(x))\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\cdot\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{*}(x)\right)+\lambda_{\max}\left(Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right)+2\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}

\displaystyle CM_{L}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|

provided that constant
C>0
is large enough. Here (i) follows from (
73
) and (ii) follows since
Q^{*}(x),Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{L}^{-1},M_{L})
for
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
by Lemma
33
.

Note also that for any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
, one has
\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}M_{L}
.
Recall the definition of
\delta_{F},\alpha_{F},\gamma_{2}(\cdot,\cdot)
from Assumption
4
.
Then for any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
that satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\leq\frac{\delta_{F}^{\alpha_{F}}}{CM_{L}\gamma_{2}(L,\sqrt{d}M_{L})}=:t_{0}

(75)

one can obtain

\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|_{F}\leq\left(CM_{L}\gamma_{2}(L,\sqrt{d}M_{L})\right)^{1/\alpha_{F}}\cdot\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|^{1/\alpha_{F}}

(76)

To see this, note that first we have
\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|_{F}<\delta_{F}
since otherwise Assumption
4
then implies that

F(x,Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))-F(x,Q^{*}(x))>\frac{\delta_{F}^{\alpha_{F}}}{\gamma_{2}(L,\sqrt{d}M_{L})}\overset{(i)}{\geq}CM_{L}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|

Here
(i)
results from (
75
).
This is a contradiction with (
74
). Next applying Assumption
4
with
\delta=\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|_{F}
and
\Delta=\sqrt{d}M_{L}
gives

\displaystyle F(x,Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))-F(x,Q^{*}(x))\geq\frac{\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|^{\alpha_{F}}}{\gamma_{2}(L,\sqrt{d}M_{L})}

which combined with (
73
) implies (
76
).

3.
x,\widetilde{x}
far apart:

for any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
such that
\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|>t_{0}
, let
K:=\lceil{\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|/t_{0}}\rceil
and

x_{k}:=\left(1-\frac{k}{K}\right)x+\frac{k}{K}\widetilde{x},\quad k=0,\ldots,K

Then
\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\right\|\leq t_{0}
, and one can apply (
76
) to see that

\displaystyle\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left\|Q^{*}(x_{k+1})-Q^{*}(x_{k})\right\|

(77)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(CM_{L}\gamma_{2}(L,\sqrt{d}M_{L})\right)^{1/\alpha_{F}}\cdot\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\right\|^{1/\alpha_{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(CM_{L}\gamma_{2}(L,\sqrt{d}M_{L})\right)^{1/\alpha_{F}}\cdot t_{0}^{1/\alpha_{F}}

\displaystyle=K\delta_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|}{t_{0}}\delta_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{L}\gamma_{2}(L,\sqrt{d}M_{L})\cdot\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|

Here the last line follows since
\delta_{F}
is a constant defined in Assumption
4
.

Finally, combine (
76
) and (
77
) to see that for any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
, one has

\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|\leq C_{H}\cdot\max\left\{\left(M_{L}\gamma_{2}(L,M_{L})\right)^{1/\alpha_{F}}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|^{1/\alpha_{F}},M_{L}\gamma_{2}(L,M_{L})\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\right\}

for some constant
C_{H}
independent of
n
(but depends on
d
).
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
6
and Corollary
9

C.1
Proof outline for uniform convergence

We start by introducing some notations. Define

\displaystyle\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,S)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})W^{2}(S,Q_{i})

(78)

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{\rho}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{\rho}\left(x,S\right)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{S}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{n}(x,S)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w(x,X_{i})W^{2}(S,Q_{i})

(79)

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w(x,X_{i})\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w(x,X_{i})dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x,S)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w(x,X_{i})d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{S}

For any
t\geq 1
, define the event
E_{0}(t)
as

\displaystyle E_{0}(t)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\|X_{i}-\mu\right\|\leq t,Q_{i}\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{t}^{-1},M_{t}),i\in[n]\right\},\quad\text{where }M_{t}:=\gamma_{\Lambda}(t)

Specifically, let
L_{\tau}=\sqrt{(1+\tau)\log n}
for
\tau\geq 0
and define

\displaystyle E_{0}=E_{0}\left(C_{\psi_{2}}L_{\tau}\right),

(80)

Under Assumption
1
and
2
, we have
\mathbb{P}(E_{0}^{c})\leq 2n^{-(1+\tau)}
.

We now give an outline of the proof strategy for our uniform convergence theory, namely, Theorems
6
and Corollary
9
.

•

Lemma
36
derives a tail probability bound for the largest eigenvalue of
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
.

•

Lemma
37
obtains uniform concentration of
\widehat{F}_{\rho}
.

•

Lemma
38
shows the uniform consistency of
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
with a potentially slow rate;

•

Lemma
40
-
42
deliver upper bounds for various quantities related to
\widehat{A}_{\rho}(x)
,
\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)
and
\widehat{\Psi}_{\rho}(x;S)
.

•

Lemma
43
arrives at the non-asymptotic
\sqrt{n}
-uniform convergence in the Frobenius norm.

•

Lemma
44
combines Lemma
36
and
43
to get a
\sqrt{n}
-moment bound for the uniform Frobenius error.

•

Lemma
45
extends the above moment of Frobenius error result to Wasserstein distance.

Theorem
6
and Corollary
9
then follow immediately by combining Lemmas
36
-
45
.

Lemma 36
.

Suppose Assumption
1
-
2
hold. If
n\geq 3
,
1\leq L\leq\sqrt{n}
, then there exist constant
C_{b},c_{b},c_{e}>0
independent of
n
such that with
t_{0}=C_{b}L
, the following holds:

•

when
\rho=0
, for any
t\geq t_{0}
, the tail probability satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)\geq t^{2}\right)\lesssim h_{0}(t)+\exp(-c_{e}n)

•

when
\rho=n^{-1}
, the tail probability satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)\geq t^{2}\right)\lesssim\begin{cases}h_{0}(t)+\exp(-c_{e}n),&t\in\left[t_{0},nt_{0}\right]\\
h_{0}(t)+\exp(-c_{e}n)\wedge h_{0}(t/n),&t\in\left(nt_{0},+\infty\right)\end{cases}

where we denote

\displaystyle h_{0}(t):=\exp\left(-\sqrt{\frac{t}{c_{b}L}}\right)+n\cdot\exp\left[-\left(\frac{t}{c_{b}L}\right)^{2/(C_{\Lambda}+2)}\right]

As a result, denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}_{L}

\displaystyle:=\left(c_{b}\vee C_{b}\right)^{2}L^{2}L_{\tau}^{4+\left(4\vee 2C_{1}\right)}

(81)

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right)<\widetilde{M}_{L}\right\}

(82)

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}_{0}^{c})\leq C_{\lambda,\tau}n^{-\tau}

(83)

for some constant
C_{\lambda,\tau}
independent of
n
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.2
.
∎

Denote
F(x,S)=\mathbb{E}\widetilde{F}_{n}(x,S)
.

Lemma 37
.

Instate the notations and assumptions in Theorem
6
and Lemma
36
. Then for any
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x\in B_\mu(L)}{S \in\cS_d( 0,\tM_L)}}\left|\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,S)-F(x,S)\right|=C_{F,\tau}\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}

with probability greater than
1-c_{F,\tau}n^{-\tau}
for constants
C_{F,\tau},c_{F,\tau}
independent of
n
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.3
.
∎

Lemma 38
.

Instate the notations and assumptions in Theorem
6
and Lemma
37
. Let
1\leq L=O(\sqrt{\log n})
. Let
\rho\in\left\{0,1/n\right\}
. Then for any
\tau
, there exist an event
\widetilde{E}_{1}\subset\widetilde{E}_{0}
such that

•

\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}_{1}^{c})\leq c_{\delta,\tau}n^{-\tau}

•

under
\widetilde{E}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=C_{\delta,\tau}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n^{1/(2\alpha_{F})}}

(84)

for constants
C_{\delta,\tau},c_{\delta,\tau}
independent of
n
.

As a result, under
\widetilde{E}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\in{\cal S}_{d}\left((C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L})^{-1},C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L}\right),\quad\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)

(85)

for some constant
C_{\mathrm{slow}}>0
independent of
n
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.4
.
∎

Remark 39
.

We remind readers that in Lemma
38
,
\alpha_{F}\geq 1
is defined in Assumption
4
. Therefore, Lemma
38
might only lead to a uniform convergence rate slower than
\sqrt{n}
. For example, it is shown in Lemma
34
that
\alpha_{F}=2
when
X
and
Q
are independent, which results by (
84
) in a uniform convergence rate of
n^{1/4}
. Such a show rate is not enough to derive the asymptotic null distribution of our test statistic in Theorem
15
. Therefore, we apply a finer analysis in the following part of proof to further boost the uniform convergence rate to
n^{1/2}
.

Lemma 40
.

Instate the notations and assumptions in Theorem
6
and Lemma
38
. Then there exists an event
E_{2,1}\subset\widetilde{E}_{1}
with probability greater than
1-c_{\tau,1}n^{-\tau}
under which

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{\tau,1}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(86)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\leq C_{\tau,1}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(87)

\displaystyle\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x \in B_\mu(L)}{S \in\cS_d \rbr{(C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L})^{-1},C_{\mathrm{slow}} M_{L}}}}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x;S)-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x;S)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\leq C_{\tau,1}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(88)

\displaystyle\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x \in B_\mu(L)}{S \in\cS_d \rbr{(C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L})^{-1},C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L}}}}

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x;S)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\leq C_{\tau,1}\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)

(89)

for constants
C_{\tau,1},c_{\tau,1}
independent of
n
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.5
.
∎

C.2
Proof of Lemma
36

Without loss of generality, assume
\mu=0
,
\Sigma=I_{p}
,
C_{\psi_{2}}=1
and
c_{\Lambda}=1
(defined in Assumption
2
). Denote
\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)=\lambda_{\max}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right)
.

By the optimality condition for
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
, one has

\displaystyle 0=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)

Note that
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})=1
for any
x
, hence one has

\displaystyle I_{d}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})T_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)}^{Q_{i}}

Recall that
T_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)}^{Q}={\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)}^{-1/2}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{1/2}Q\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{-1/2}
, multiplying both sides of the equation with
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{1/2}
then gives

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{1/2}Q_{i}\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}

Taking the largest eigenvalue on both sides, one has

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\right|\cdot\left\|\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{1/2}Q_{i}\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\right|\cdot\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{1/2}(x)

which implies

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|1+(x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right|\cdot\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\right]^{2}

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)\geq t^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq t/2\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{2<\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq t/n\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq t/2\right)}_{\mathbf{(I)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(2<\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\right)\wedge\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq t/n\right)}_{\mathbf{(II)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n\right)}_{(\mathbf{III})}

Claim 1
.

Suppose Assumption
1
-
2
hold with
\mu=0
,
\Sigma=I_{p}
,
C_{\psi_{2}}=1
and
c_{\Lambda}=1
. If
n\geq 3
,
1\leq L\leq\sqrt{n}
.
then for any
t\geq 6L
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>2\right\}\lesssim\exp(-c_{e}n)

(97)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq t\right\}\lesssim h_{0}(t)

(98)

where

\displaystyle h_{0}(t):=\exp\left(-\sqrt{\frac{t}{c_{b}L}}\right)+n\cdot\exp\left[-\left(\frac{t}{c_{b}L}\right)^{2/(C_{\Lambda}+2)}\right]

for some constants
c_{b},c_{e}>0
independent of
n
.

See Appendix
C.2.1
for the proof.

Combining above results, one can obtain that

•

If
\rho=0
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{(II)}+\mathbf{(III)}\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>2\right)

As a result, for any
t\geq 6L
, the tail probability satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)\geq t^{2}\right)\lesssim h_{0}(t)+\exp(-c_{e}n)

•

If
\rho=n^{-1}
, then
\mathbf{(III)}=0
. As a results, the tail probability satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)\geq t^{2}\right)\lesssim\begin{cases}h_{0}(t)+\exp(-c_{e}n),&t\in\left[t_{0},nt_{0}\right]\\
h_{0}(t)+\exp(-c_{e}n)\wedge h_{0}(t/n),&t\in\left(nt_{0},+\infty\right)\end{cases}

with
t_{0}=6L
.

Finally, set

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{L}=\left(C_{b}\vee c_{b}\right)L_{\tau}^{2+\left(2\vee 2C_{1}\right)}L

Note that
\tilde{t}_{L}\geq t_{0}
, hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(x)\geq\tilde{t}_{L}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim h_{0}(\tilde{t}_{L})+\exp(-c_{e}n)

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-(1+\tau)}+\exp(-c_{e}n)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tau}n^{-(1+\tau)}

for some constant
C_{\tau}
independent of
n
(but depends on
\tau
).

This finishes the proof of Lemma
36
.

C.2.1
Proof of Claim
1

The first inequality follows by noticing that
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\top}+\rho I_{p}
with
\rho\in\left\{0,1/n\right\}
. Apply triangle inequality, one can obtain that for
n\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>2\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1/2\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1/12\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1/12\right)

Under the sub-Gaussian assumption in Assumption
1
, one can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}-I_{p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1/12\right\}\lesssim\exp(-cn)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1/12\right\}\lesssim\exp(-cn)

Now we move on to the second inequality. Note that

\displaystyle\quad\ \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|x\right\|\cdot\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\left\|x\right\|\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}+\left(\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|x\right\|\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}

Therefore, for any
L\geq 1
and
t>0
, one has

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|x-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\right)\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(1+L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{1}{1+L}t\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|x\right\|\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{L}{1+L}t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(1+L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{2L}\right\}}_{\mathbf{(A)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|+L\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{2}\right\}}_{\mathbf{(B)}}

Analysis for (A):
for any
1\leq L\leq\sqrt{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{(A)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{1}{1+L/\sqrt{n}+1/n}\cdot\frac{t}{2L}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{L\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{L/\sqrt{n}}{1+L/\sqrt{n}+1/n}\cdot\frac{t}{2L}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{1/n}{1+L/\sqrt{n}+1/n}\cdot\frac{t}{2L}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{1}{3}\cdot\frac{t}{2L}\right\}+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{1/\sqrt{n}}{3}\cdot\frac{t}{2L}\right\}}_{\mathbf{A_{1}}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{1/n}{3}\cdot\frac{t}{2L}\right\}}_{\mathbf{A_{2}}}

•

\mathbf{A_{1}}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A_{1}}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}

•

\mathbf{A_{2}}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A_{2}}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{n}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}

Hence for
t\geq 6L
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{(A)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{n}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}+\exp\left(-c\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right)

Analysis for (B):

\displaystyle\mathbf{(B)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{L\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{\sqrt{n}L}{\sqrt{n}L+1}\frac{t}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}L+1}\frac{t}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{4L}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{4\sqrt{n}L}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{4L}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{4nL}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{4L}}\right\}

Hence for
t\geq 4L
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{(B)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\right\|\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{4nL}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{4L}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-ct/L)+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{4L}}\right\}

Combining results for
\mathbf{(A)}
and
\mathbf{(B)}
, one can obtain that for
t\geq 6L
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbf{(A)}+\mathbf{(B)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(-c\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right)+\exp(-ct/L)+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{4L}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(-c\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}(Q_{i})\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{6L}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}(Q_{i})\geq\sqrt{\frac{t}{4L}}\right\}

Note that for any
s\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{(1\vee\left\|X_{i}\right\|)^{C_{\Lambda}/2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|X_{i}\right\|\geq s^{2/C_{\Lambda}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\cdot\exp\left(-cs^{4/C_{\Lambda}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|X_{i}\right\|\lambda_{\max}(Q_{i})^{1/2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|X_{i}\right\|(1\vee\left\|X_{i}\right\|)^{C_{\Lambda}/2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|X_{i}\right\|^{1+C_{\Lambda}/2}\geq s\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\cdot\exp\left(-cs^{4/(C_{\Lambda}+2)}\right)

Therefore, for any
t\geq 6L
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{(A)}+\mathbf{(B)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(-c\sqrt{\frac{t}{L}}\right)+n\cdot\exp\left[-c\left(t/L\right)^{2/C_{\Lambda}}\right]+n\cdot\exp\left[-c\left(t/L\right)^{2/(C_{\Lambda}+2)}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(-c\sqrt{\frac{t}{L}}\right)+n\cdot\exp\left[-c\left(t/L\right)^{2/(C_{\Lambda}+2)}\right]

The proof of Claim
1
is complete by adjusting constant
c
here.

C.3
Proof of Lemma
37

Without loss of generality, assume
\mu=0
,
\Sigma=I_{p}
,
C_{\psi_{2}}=1
.
Recall notation
{\cal S}_{d}(a,b):=\left\{A\in{\cal S}_{d}:aI_{d}\prec A\prec bI_{d}\right\}
. Denote parameter
\theta=(x,S)
and parameter space
\Theta=B_{\mu}(L)\times{\cal S}_{d}(0,\widetilde{M}_{L})
. Let
F(\theta)
denote
F(x,S)
and similarly for other functions of
(x,S)
.
By triangle inequality, one can obtain that

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\widehat{F}_{\rho}(\theta)-F(\theta)\right|\leq\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\widehat{F}_{\rho}(\theta)-\widetilde{F}_{n}(\theta)\right|}_{\zeta_{1}}+\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\widetilde{F}_{n}(\theta)-F(\theta)\right|}_{\zeta_{2}}

(99)

For any
t_{1},t_{2}>0
, define
C_{\sup}(t_{1},t_{2}):=\sup\left\{W^{2}(Q,S):Q\in{\cal S}_{d}(0,t_{1}),S\in{\cal S}_{d}(0,t_{2})\right\}
. By Lemma
21
, one has

\displaystyle C_{\sup}(t_{1},t_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim t_{1}+t_{2}

(100)

Here dimension
d
is viewed as a fixed constant and absorbed into
\lesssim
.

•

\zeta_{1}
:
under event
E_{0}
(defined in (
80
)), one has

–

\left\|X_{i}-\mu\right\|\leq L_{\tau}=\sqrt{(1+\tau)\log n}
for
i=1,...,n
.

–

W^{2}(Q_{i},S)\lesssim M_{L_{\tau}}\vee\widetilde{M}_{L}
for any
S\in{\cal S}_{d}(0,\widetilde{M}_{L})
and
i\in[n]
.

Lemma
28
then implies that

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(M_{L_{\tau}}\vee\widetilde{M}_{L}\right)\frac{LL_{\tau}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}

(101)

with probability greater than
1-O\left(n^{-(1+\tau)}\right)
.

•

\zeta_{2}
can be bounded by truncation and the uniform concentration in Lemma
29
. Let
\widetilde{L}\geq 1
be a parameter to be specified later, and let
M_{\widetilde{L}}:=\gamma_{\Lambda}(\widetilde{L})
. Then Assumption
2
implies that a.s.

X\in B_{\mu}(\widetilde{L})\implies Q\in{\cal S}_{d}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)

(102)

Let
\widetilde{X}
be a truncated form of
X
defined as

\widetilde{X}:=\widetilde{X}(\widetilde{L})=X\bm{1}(\left\|X\right\|\leq\widetilde{L})

(103)

Let
\widetilde{X}_{>}:=X-\widetilde{X}
. By definition, one has
X=\widetilde{X}+\widetilde{X}_{>}
,
\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|\leq\widetilde{L}
and

\widetilde{X}_{>}=\begin{cases}0,&\left\|X\right\|\leq\widetilde{L}\\
X,&\left\|X\right\|>\widetilde{L}\end{cases}

(104)

As a result, one can obtain the following decomposition.

\displaystyle w(x,X)W^{2}(S,Q)

\displaystyle=\left(1+x^{\top}X\right)W^{2}(S,Q)

\displaystyle=\left(1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}\right)W^{2}(S,Q)+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{>}W^{2}(S,Q)

\displaystyle=\left(1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}\right)W^{2}(S,Q)\bm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}\right)W^{2}(S,Q)\bm{1}\left(Q\notin{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{>}W^{2}(S,Q)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\left(1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}\right)W^{2}(S,Q)\bm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+W^{2}(S,Q)\bm{1}\left(Q\notin{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{>}W^{2}(S,Q)

Here (i) follows from (
102
). Indeed, if
Q\notin{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)
, then as a result of (
102
), one has
\left\|X\right\|>\widetilde{L}
which then implies
\widetilde{X}=0
.

As a consequence, one can obtain then following decomposition of
\widetilde{F}_{n}(\theta)-F(\theta)
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{n}(\theta)-F(\theta)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}(\theta)+\alpha_{2}(\theta)+\alpha_{3}(\theta),\quad\text{where}

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{i}\right)W^{2}(S,Q_{i})\bm{1}\left(Q_{i}\in{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left(1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}\right)W^{2}(S,Q)\bm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{i,>}W^{2}(S,Q_{i})-\mathbb{E}x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{>}W^{2}(S,Q)

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W^{2}(S,Q_{i})\bm{1}\left(Q_{i}\notin{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}W^{2}(S,Q)\bm{1}\left(Q\notin{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

Then triangle inequality gives

\zeta_{2}\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{2}(\theta)\right|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{3}(\theta)\right|

(105)

–

\alpha_{2}(\theta),\alpha_{3}(\theta)
:
\sup\left|\alpha_{2}(\theta)\right|
and
\sup\left|\alpha_{3}(\theta)\right|
can be upper bounded as in Claim
2
. The proof is deferred to Appendix
C.3.1
.

Claim 2
.

There exists constant
\widetilde{C}
independent of
n
such that
by taking
\widetilde{L}=\widetilde{C}C_{\psi_{2}}\sqrt{(1+\tau)\log n}
, the following inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{2}(\theta)\right|\vee\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{3}(\theta)\right|

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\widetilde{M}_{L}\widetilde{L}+\widetilde{L}^{1+C_{\Lambda}}}{n^{1+\tau}}\cdot L\right)

holds with probability greater than
1-O\left(n^{-\tau}\right)
.

–

\alpha_{1}(\theta)
: Apply triangle inequality to see that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right|

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right|-\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right|}_{b_{1}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right|}_{b_{2}}

(106)

After truncation,
\alpha_{1}(\theta)
is uniformly bounded. Hence
b_{1}
can be upper bounded by exploiting the bounded difference property (Lemma
31
). For
b_{2}
, we first show that
W^{2}(Q,S)
is only Hölder continuous in
S
which then implies Hölder (rather than Lipschitz) continuity of the corresponding sub-Gaussian norm. By generalizing Dudley’s integral inequality via chaining in Lemma
29
, we arrive at an upper bound for
b_{2}
. The results are summarized below in
in Claim
3
whose proof is deferred to Appendix
C.3.2
.

Claim 3
.

Instate the notations and assumptions in Lemma
38
and Claim
2
.

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right|=O\left(\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log\widetilde{M}_{L}}\right)

with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-(1+\tau)}\right)
.

–

combine Claim
2
, Claim
3
and (
105
) to see that

\zeta_{2}=O\left(\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

(107)

with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-\tau}\right)
.

•

Combining results for
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\widehat{F}_{\rho}(\theta)-F(\theta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

with probability greater than
1-O\left(n^{-\tau}\right)
.

The proof of Lemma
37
is now complete.

C.3.1
Proof of Claim
2

Without loss generality, assume
\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\left\|\left\|X\right\|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq 1
.

Proof for
\alpha_{2}(\theta)
: a crude upper bounded suffices here. By triangle inequality, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{2}(\theta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\mathbb{E}x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{>}W^{2}(S,Q)\right|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{i,>}W^{2}(S,Q_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left\|x\right\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|\cdot W^{2}(S,Q)+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|x\right\|\cdot\left\|\widetilde{X}_{i,>}\right\|\cdot W^{2}(S,Q_{i})

Therefore, for any
t>0
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{2}(\theta)\right|\geq t\right\}\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left\|x\right\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|\cdot W^{2}(S,Q)\geq t\right\}}_{=:\mathbf{I}(t)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|x\right\|\cdot\left\|\widetilde{X}_{i,>}\right\|\cdot W^{2}(S,Q_{i})>0\right\}}_{=:\mathbf{II}(t)}

I(t):
Lemma
21
and Assumption
2
imply that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}W^{2}(S,Q)\lesssim\widetilde{M}_{L}+\gamma_{\Lambda}(\left\|X\right\|)

which further gives

\displaystyle\mathbf{(I)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|\left(\widetilde{M}_{L}+\left\|X\right\|^{C_{\Lambda}}\right)\geq t/(Lc)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|\geq\frac{t}{2cL\widetilde{M}_{L}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|\left\|X\right\|^{C_{\Lambda}}\geq t/(2Lc)\right\}

By the definition of
\widetilde{X}_{>}
, one can obtain that
\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|\left\|X\right\|^{C_{\Lambda}}=\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|^{1+C_{\Lambda}}
and for any
s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|\geq\widetilde{L}+s\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\widetilde{L}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-s^{2}\right)

Therefore, one can obtain that for any
\widetilde{L}\geq 1
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp(-\widetilde{L}^{2})\int_{0}^{\infty}(\widetilde{L}+s)\exp(-s^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-\widetilde{L}^{2})\widetilde{L}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\widetilde{X}_{>}\right\|^{1+C_{\Lambda}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp(-\widetilde{L}^{2})\int_{0}^{\infty}(\widetilde{L}+s)^{1+C_{\Lambda}}\exp(-s^{2})ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-\widetilde{L}^{2})\widetilde{L}^{1+C_{\Lambda}}

Therefore, by taking
\widetilde{L}=O(L_{\tau})
, there exists constant
C_{I}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{I(t)}=0,\quad\forall t\geq C_{I}n^{-(1+\tau)}\cdot\left(\widetilde{M}_{L}+M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)L\widetilde{L}

II:
Note that
\left\|\widetilde{X}_{i,>}\right\|>0
if and only only if
\left\|X_{i}\right\|>\widetilde{L}
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{(II)}

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\left\|X_{i}\right\|>\widetilde{L}\right\|\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\cdot\exp(-\widetilde{L}^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\tau}

Combining (I), (II),
there exists constant
\widetilde{C}_{2}>0
such that for any
s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{2}(\theta)\right|\geq n^{-(1+\tau)}\widetilde{C}_{2}L\widetilde{L}\left(\widetilde{M}_{L}+\widetilde{L}^{C_{\Lambda}}\right)\right\}\lesssim n^{-\tau}

Proof for
\alpha_{3}(\theta)
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W^{2}(S,Q_{i})\bm{1}\left(Q_{i}\notin{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}W^{2}(S,Q)\bm{1}\left(Q\notin{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

Following a similar argument to the one above, there exists constant
\widetilde{C}_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{3}(\theta)\right|\geq n^{-(1+\tau)}\widetilde{C}_{3}L(\widetilde{M}_{L}+\widetilde{L}^{C_{\Lambda}})\right\}\lesssim n^{-\tau}

Finally, combining above results on
\alpha_{2}(\theta)
and
\alpha_{3}(\theta)
proves Claim
2
.

C.3.2
Proof of Claim
3

Recall that we assume without loss of generality that
C_{\psi_{2}}=1
,
\mu=0
,
\Sigma=I_{p}
,
\widetilde{L}=L_{\tau}
,
M_{\widetilde{L}}=\gamma_{\Lambda}(\widetilde{L})
and
\widetilde{M}_{L}
is defined in Lemma
36
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}_{L}\asymp L^{2}L_{\tau}^{4+\left(4\vee 2C_{1}\right)}\gtrsim c_{\Lambda}(1\vee\widetilde{L})^{C_{\Lambda}}=M_{\widetilde{L}}

Denote
Z=(X,Q)
and define
\widetilde{X}
as in (
103
). Define

\displaystyle f(Z;\theta)

\displaystyle:=\left(1+(x-\mu)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}(\widetilde{X}-\mu)\right)W^{2}(S,Q)\mathbbm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

Then one has
\alpha_{1}(\theta)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(Z_{i};\theta)-\mathbb{E}f(Z;\theta)
.

Analysis of
b_{1}
:

due to truncation,
f
is uniformly bounded. To see this, note that recall the definition of
\Theta
,

\theta\in\Theta\iff x\in B_{\mu}(L),S\in{\cal S}_{d}(0,\widetilde{M}_{L})

Then for any
\theta\in\Theta
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{Z}\left|f(Z;\theta)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{Z}\left|(1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X})W^{2}(Q,S)\right|\mathbbm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}_{d}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

(108)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{Z}\left(1+\left\|x-\mu\right\|\cdot\left\|\widetilde{X}-\mu\right\|\right)\cdot\left|W^{2}(Q,S)\right|\mathbbm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}_{d}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+L\widetilde{L}\right)\cdot(M_{\widetilde{L}}+\widetilde{M}_{L})

\displaystyle\lesssim L\widetilde{L}\widetilde{M}_{L}

By Lemma
31
, one has
\left\|b_{1}(x)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\frac{L\widetilde{L}\widetilde{M}_{L}}{\sqrt{n}}
which implies

b_{1}=O\left(\frac{L\widetilde{L}\widetilde{M}_{L}L_{\tau}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

(109)

with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-(1+\tau)}\right)
for some absolute constant
C>0
.

Analysis of
b_{2}
:

to apply Lemma
29
, we follow the steps below.

•

first we define a metric
d
on
\Theta
by

d(\theta,\widetilde{\theta})=\max\left\{\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|_{2},\left\|S-\widetilde{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\}

for any
\theta=(x,S)
and
\widetilde{S}=(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})
. Since
{\cal S}_{d}(0,\widetilde{M}_{L})
is a subset of
\left\{S\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\left|S_{ij}\right|\leq\widetilde{M}_{L}\right\}
(by noticing that for any
S\in{\cal S}^{+}_{\widetilde{M}_{L}}
, one has
0\leq S_{ii}\leq\widetilde{M}_{L}
and
\left|S_{ij}\right|\leq\sqrt{S_{ii}S_{jj}}\leq\widetilde{M}_{L}
), the diameter
D
and metric entropy of
\Theta
can be upper bounded by

D\leq\max\left\{\mathrm{diam}(B_{\mu}(\widetilde{L})),\mathrm{diam}({\cal S}_{d}(\widetilde{M}_{L}^{-1},\widetilde{M}_{L}))\right\}\lesssim L\vee\widetilde{M}_{L}\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\widetilde{M}_{L}

(110)

and

\displaystyle\log N(\epsilon;\Theta)

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(N(\epsilon;B_{\mu}(\widetilde{L}))\cdot N(\epsilon;{\cal S}^{+}_{\widetilde{M}_{L}})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{+}\left(\frac{L\vee\widetilde{M}_{L}}{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\log^{+}\left(\frac{\widetilde{M}_{L}}{\epsilon}\right)

(111)

Here both (i) and (ii) arise due to
\widetilde{M}_{L}\gtrsim L
.

•

Next, let us consider the sub-Gaussian norm of
\alpha_{1}(\theta)-\alpha_{1}(\widetilde{\theta})
as (
42
) in Lemma
29
. By (
45
) in Lemma
29
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\alpha_{1}(\theta)-\alpha_{1}(\widetilde{\theta})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\left|f(Z;\theta)-f(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

(112)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\left|f(Z;x,S)-f(Z;\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\underbrace{\left\|\left|f(Z;x,S)-f(Z;x,\widetilde{S})\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}}_{\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\underbrace{\left\|\left|f(Z;x,\widetilde{S})-f(Z;\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}}_{\beta_{2}}

–

\beta_{1}
:
Let
g(Q;S):=(Q^{1/2}SQ^{1/2})^{1/2}
. Then for any
Q\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}})
,

\displaystyle\left\|g(Q;S)-g(Q;\widetilde{S})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|(Q^{1/2}SQ^{1/2})^{1/2}-(Q^{1/2}\widetilde{S}Q^{1/2})^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|Q^{1/2}SQ^{1/2}-Q^{1/2}\widetilde{S}Q^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|S-\widetilde{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{1/2}\left\|Q\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq M_{\widetilde{L}}^{1/2}\left\|S-\widetilde{S}\right\|_{F}^{1/2}

Here in the second inequality, we exploit the following the Hölder continuity of matrix square root in
Wihler, (2009)
;
Carlsson, (2018)
.

\left\|A^{1/2}-B^{1/2}\right\|_{F}\leq C_{d}\left\|A-B\right\|_{F}^{1/2}

where
C_{d}
is a constant only depending on dimension
d
. Hence for any
\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left|f(X,Q;x,S)-f(X,Q;x,\widetilde{S})\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left|1+x^{\top}\widetilde{X}\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\cdot\left\|\left(g(Q;S)-g(Q;\widetilde{S})\right)\cdot\mathbbm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}_{d}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\left\|x\right\|\cdot\left\|\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\right)\cdot M_{\widetilde{L}}^{1/2}\left\|S-\widetilde{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim L\cdot M_{\widetilde{L}}^{1/2}\left\|S-\widetilde{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{1/2}

(113)

–

\beta_{2}
: for any
\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta
, one has

\displaystyle\beta_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|(x-\widetilde{x})^{\top}\widetilde{X}W^{2}(Q,S)\mathbbm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}_{d}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\cdot\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}+\widetilde{M}_{L}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{M}_{L}\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|_{2}

(114)

Here (i) follows from (
100
).

–

As a result of (
113
) and (
114
), one can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|f(Z;\theta)-f(Z;\widetilde{\theta})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim LM_{\widetilde{L}}^{1/2}d(\theta,\widetilde{\theta})^{1/2}+\widetilde{M}_{L}d(\theta,\widetilde{\theta})

\displaystyle\lesssim L\widetilde{M}_{L}\left(d(\theta,\widetilde{\theta})^{1/2}\vee d(\theta,\widetilde{\theta})\right)

(115)

Combining (
112
) and (
115
) then gives

\displaystyle\left\|\alpha_{1}(\theta)-\alpha_{1}(\widetilde{\theta})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(d(\theta,\widetilde{\theta})^{1/2}\vee d(\theta,\widetilde{\theta})\right)

Hence
\tau(\epsilon)
in Lemma
29
can be chosen as

\displaystyle\tau(\epsilon)=\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\epsilon^{1/2}\vee\epsilon\right),\quad K=C_{f}L\widetilde{M}_{L}

(116)

•

by Lemma
29
, one has

\displaystyle b_{2}:=\mathbb{E}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(Z;\theta)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\frac{KD}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log^{+}D}}_{\zeta_{1}}+\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}\left|\alpha_{1}(Z;\theta)\right|}_{\zeta_{2}}

–

\zeta_{1}
: by (
44
) in Lemma
29
and (
110
), one can obtain

\zeta_{1}\lesssim\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log\widetilde{M}_{L}}

(117)

–

\zeta_{2}
: For any
\theta\in\Theta
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|f(Z;\theta)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left|f(Z;\theta)\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left|1+(x-\mu)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}(\widetilde{X}-\mu)\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\sup_{S\in{\cal S}_{d}(0,\widetilde{M}_{L})}\left[W^{2}(Q,S)\mathbbm{1}\left(Q\in{\cal S}_{d}\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+\left\|x-\mu\right\|\left\|\left\|\widetilde{X}-\mu\right\|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\right)\cdot\left(M_{\widetilde{L}}+\widetilde{M}_{L}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim L\widetilde{M}_{L}

Therefore for any
\theta\in\Theta
, one has
\left\|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim L\widetilde{M}_{L}/\sqrt{n}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\alpha_{1}(Z;\theta)\right|\lesssim\left\|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}}{\sqrt{n}}

Take supremum over
\theta\in\Theta
to see that

\zeta_{2}\lesssim\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}}{\sqrt{n}}

(118)

–

As a result of (
117
) and (
118
), one can obtain

\displaystyle b_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log\widetilde{M}_{L}}

(119)

Finally, combine (
109
) and (
119
) to see that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\alpha_{1}(\theta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq b_{1}+b_{2}\lesssim C\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log\widetilde{M}_{L}}

(120)

with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-(1+\tau)}\right)
. The proof of Claim
3
is then complete.

C.4
Proof of Lemma
38

Without loss of generality, assume
\mu=0
,
\Sigma=I_{p}
and
C_{\psi_{2}}=1
in Assumption
1
-Assumption
2
.
To prove the convergence rate of
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}
(
84
), observe that for any
\delta_{n},\epsilon_{n}>0
, one has

\displaystyle\quad\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\supset}\left\{\inf_{S:\left\|S-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta_{n}}\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,S)<\inf_{S:\left\|S-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\delta_{n}}\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,S),\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\supset}\left\{\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,Q^{*}(x))<\inf_{S:\left\|S-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\delta_{n}}\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,S),\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}\cap\left\{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\preceq\widetilde{M}_{L}I_{d},\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\supset}\left\{\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,Q^{*}(x))<\inf_{S:\underset{S\preceq\widetilde{M}_{L}I_{d}}{\left\|S-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\delta_{n}}}\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,S),\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}\cap\left\{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\preceq\widetilde{M}_{L}I_{d},\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}

\displaystyle\supset\underbrace{\left\{\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x\in B_\mu(L)}{S \preceq\tM_L I_d}}\left|\widehat{F}_{\rho}(x,S)-F(x,S)\right|\leq\epsilon_{n}\right\}}_{=:{\cal E}_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\cap\underbrace{\left\{F(x,Q^{*}(x))\leq\inf_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{S:\Fnorm{S-Q^*(x)} \geq\delta_n}{S \preceq\tM_L I_d}}F(x,S)-3\epsilon_{n},\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}}_{=:{\cal E}_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\cap\underbrace{\left\{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\preceq\widetilde{M}_{L}I_{d},\forall x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}}_{=\widetilde{E}_{0}\text{ in Lemma }\ref{lem: bdd}}

(121)

Here (i) and (iii) follows since
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
is the minimizer of
F_{n,\rho}(x,\cdot)
, (ii) is obtained by setting
S=Q^{*}(x)
.

With (
121
) in place, it suffices to choose appropriate
\delta_{n},\epsilon_{n}
such that
{\cal E}_{1}\cap{\cal E}_{2}
occurs with high probability and define
E_{2}={\cal E}_{1}\cap{\cal E}_{2}\cap E_{0}
. To this end, we first find the uniform convergence rate
\epsilon_{n}
so that
\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{1}^{c})\lesssim n^{-\tau}
. Next,
\delta_{n}
is set based on
\epsilon_{n}
and properties of
F
so that
\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{2}^{c})\lesssim n^{-\tau}
. For
{\cal E}_{3}
, we already showed in (
82
) that
\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{3}^{c})\lesssim n^{-\tau}
. Combining results on
{\cal E}_{1},{\cal E}_{2}
and
{\cal E}_{3}
, the proof is then finished.

Analysis of
{\cal E}_{1}
:

Denote
\epsilon_{0}=\delta_{F}^{\alpha_{F}}/\gamma_{F}(L,\sqrt{d}\widetilde{M}_{L})
(
\delta_{F}
and
\gamma_{F}(\cdot,\cdot)
are defined in Assumption
4
) and set

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n}

\displaystyle:=C_{F,\tau}\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\wedge\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{3}

Note that for
L=O\left(\sqrt{\log n}\right)
,
C_{F,\tau}\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\epsilon_{0}/3
for large
n
.
Therefore, Lemma
37
implies that for any
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{{\cal E}_{1}\right\}\geq 1-c_{\delta,\tau}n^{-\tau}

for some constant
c_{\delta,\tau}>0
independent of
n
.

Analysis of
{\cal E}_{2}
:

Note that for any
x\in B_{\mu}(L)
and
S\preceq\widetilde{M}_{L}I_{d}
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|S-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{d}\max\left\{\left\|S\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\left\|Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}\leq\sqrt{d}\widetilde{M}_{L}

Hence one has

\displaystyle\inf_{S:\underset{S\preceq\widetilde{M}_{L}I_{d}}{\left\|S-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\delta_{n}}}F(x,S)-F(x,Q^{*}(x))\geq\inf_{S:\delta_{n}\leq\left\|S-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{d}\widetilde{M}_{L}}F(x,S)-F(x,Q^{*}(x))

Set

\displaystyle\delta_{n}:=\left[3\epsilon_{n}\gamma_{F}(L,\sqrt{d}\widetilde{M}_{L})\right]^{1/\alpha_{F}}

By definition,
\delta_{n}\leq\delta_{F}
.
Therefore, Assumption
4
implies that
\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{2})=1
for
n
large enough.

Analysis of
{\cal E}_{3}
:

Note that
{\cal E}_{3}=\widetilde{E}_{0}
which is defined in Lemma
36
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{3}^{c})\leq C_{\lambda,\tau}n^{-\tau}

Combining
{\cal E}_{1},{\cal E}_{2},{\cal E}_{3}
:

Finally, combining results on
{\cal E}_{1},{\cal E}_{2}
and
{\cal E}_{3}
above, one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{\delta,\tau}\left[\frac{L\widetilde{M}_{L}^{3}\gamma_{F}(L,\sqrt{d}\widetilde{M}_{L})}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{1/\alpha_{F}}\right)\geq 1-c_{\delta,\tau}n^{-\tau}

for constants
C_{\delta,\tau},c_{\delta,\tau}
independent of
n
.
Note that by definition,
L,\widetilde{M}_{L},\gamma_{F}(L,\sqrt{d}\widetilde{M}_{L})\lesssim\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)
, one can then arrive at (
84
).

Finally, Eq. (
85
) follows by noticing that
\delta_{F}\wedge\delta_{n}=o\left(M_{L}\right)
.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
38
.

C.5
Proof of Lemma

Without loss of generality, assume

,

and

in Assumption

-Assumption

.

. Hence we can assume without loss of generality that

almost surely with

(122)

\displaystyle Q,Q^{*}(X)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}(E_{0})

\displaystyle=1

where we remind reader that

).

Proof of (
86
):

one can obtain the following decomposition.

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)\right\|_{F}\leq\underbrace{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)\right\|_{F}-\mathbb{E}\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)\right\|_{F}}_{a_{1}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)\right\|_{F}}_{a_{2}}

.

Analysis of
a_{1}
:
By (

), one has almost surely that for any

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+\left\|x-\mu\right\|\cdot\left\|X-\mu\right\|\right)\cdot\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d((M_{L})^{-1}, M_{L})}}\left\|T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}L\widetilde{L}M_{\widetilde{L}}^{1/2}M_{L}^{3/2}

Here (i) follows by noticing that

\displaystyle\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d((M_{L})^{-1}, M_{L})}}\left\|T^{Q}_{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d((M_{L})^{-1}, M_{L})}}\left\|S^{-1/2}(S^{1/2}QS^{1/2})^{1/2}S^{-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq M_{\widetilde{L}}^{1/2}M_{L}^{3/2}

(124)

with probability at least

.

Analysis of
a_{2}
:
to apply Lemma

, we follow the steps below.

•

First, we consider the sub-Gaussian norm of

. For any

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left\|\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)-\widetilde{A}_{n}(\widetilde{x})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

(125)

Note that

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)-\widetilde{A}_{n}(\widetilde{x})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[w(x,X_{i})\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}-I_{d}\right)-w(\widetilde{x},X_{i})\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})}-I_{d}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\underbrace{\left\|\left\|w(x,X)\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})}-I_{d}\right)-w(\widetilde{x},X)\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}}_{b_{2}}

Here (i) follows from (

.

–

almost surely. Here (i) arises from Lemma

) and (

\zeta_{2}

Proof of (
87
):

one can obtain the following decomposition.

Analysis of
a_{3}
:
By (

x\in B_{\mu}(L)
,

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|\phi(Z;x)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim L\widetilde{L}\cdot\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d(M_{L}^{-1} , M_{L})}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{Q}_{S}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim L\widetilde{L}\mathrm{poly}\left(M_{L},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)

By Lemma
31
, one then can obtain

\left\|a_{3}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\frac{L\widetilde{L}\mathrm{poly}\left(M_{L},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(134)

Analysis of
a_{4}
:
to apply Lemma
29
, we follow the steps below.

•

First, let us consider the sub-Gaussian norm of
\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)\right|\!\right|\!\right|-\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(\widetilde{x})-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(\widetilde{x})\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|\phi(Z;x)-\phi(Z;\widetilde{x})\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(x)}-w(\widetilde{x},X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(x)}-w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}}_{b_{3}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})}-w(\widetilde{x},X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}}_{b_{4}}

–

b_{3}
: we have for any
x,\widetilde{x}\in B_{\mu}(L)
,

\displaystyle b_{3}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\left\|\left|\!\left|\!\left|w(x,X)d^{2}T^{Q}_{Q^{\prime}}\cdot(Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x}))\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim L\left\|X-\mu\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\cdot\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d(M_{L}^{-1} , M_{L})}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{Q}_{S}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

(

, Theorem 5.3)
for some

that lies between

and

.

–

\displaystyle b_{4}

), (

), (

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot L\mathrm{poly}\left(M_{L},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\left(\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|+\left\|Q^{*}(x)-Q^{*}(\widetilde{x})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot L\mathrm{poly}\left(M_{L},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)\left(\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|\vee\left\|x-\widetilde{x}\right\|^{1/\alpha_{F}}\right)

C.6
Proof of Lemma
41

As argued at the beginning of Appendix
C.5
, we can assume without loss of generality that
\left\|X-\mu\right\|\leq\widetilde{L}
almost surely with
\widetilde{L}
defined in (
80
). As a result, we have almost surely that

\displaystyle\left\|X-\mu\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{L}

(151)

\displaystyle Q,Q^{*}(X)

\displaystyle\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}})

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}(E_{0})

\displaystyle=1

where
E_{0}:=\left\{\left\|X_{i}-\mu\right\|\leq L,Q_{i}\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{\widetilde{L}}^{-1},M_{\widetilde{L}}),i\in[n]\right\}
.

With boundedness condition (
151
) in place, Lemma
21
implies the following upper bounds

\displaystyle\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d \left( (C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L})^{-1},C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L} \right)}}\left\|T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{poly}\left(M_{L},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)

\displaystyle\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d \left( (C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L})^{-1},C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L} \right)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{Q}_{S}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{poly}\left(M_{L},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)

\displaystyle\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{Q \in\cS_d(M_{\tL}^{-1},M_{\tL})}{S \in\cS_d \left( (C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L})^{-1},C_{\mathrm{slow}}M_{L} \right)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{Q}_{S}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{poly}\left(M_{L},M_{\widetilde{L}}\right)

almost surely. Applying Lemma
28
then gives the desired results.

Proof of Lemma

):

Here (i) follows from Lemma

and Lemma

Proof of (

):

apply the triangle inequality to see that

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x \in B_\mu(L)}{S \in\cS_d \left( (2M_L)^{-1},2M_L \right)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widehat{\Psi}_{\rho}(x,S)-\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x,S)\right|\!\right|\!\right|+\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x \in B_\mu(L)}{S \in\cS_d \left( (2M_L)^{-1},2M_L \right)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x,S)-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x,S)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle+\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x \in B_\mu(L)}{S \in\cS_d \left( (2M_L)^{-1},2M_L \right)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x,S)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}+\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)

Here (i) follows from Lemma

and Lemma

.

Proof of (

), (

):

By the remark after Assumption

and the eigenvalue stability inequality, one can obtain

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in B_{\mu(L)}}\lambda_{\min}\left(-\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{x\in B_{\mu(L)}}\lambda_{\min}\left(-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)\right)-\sup_{x\in B_{\mu(L)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)\right|\!\right|\!\right|-\sup_{x\in B_{\mu(L)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)-\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}

, Assumption

and applying Lemma

, Lemma

.

and (

) follows from (

).

C.8
Proof of Lemma
43

Recall definitions of
Q^{*}(x)
and
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
that

\displaystyle Q^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{S\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}}\mathbb{E}\left[w(x,X)W^{2}(S,Q)\right]

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{S\in{\cal S}_{d}^{++}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})W^{2}(S,Q_{i})

First, the differential properties of
W^{2}
(Lemma
21
) imply the following optimality conditions for
Q^{*}(x)
and
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=0

(152)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=0

(153)

Next, one can apply Lemma
21
again to get the 2nd order Taylor expansion of (
153
) around
Q^{*}(x)
as follows.

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q_{i}}\cdot\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})d^{2}T_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}^{Q_{i}}\cdot\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right)^{\otimes 2}

where
\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)
lies between
Q^{*}(x)
and
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
. Rearranging then gives

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)=\left(-\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}}_{=\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}-I_{d}\right)}_{=\widehat{A}_{\rho}(x)}

(154)

\displaystyle+\left(-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}}_{=\widehat{\Psi}_{\rho}\left(x;\widetilde{Q}_{n,\rho}(x)\right)}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x))^{\otimes 2}

Taking Frobenius norm on both sides of (
154
) gives the following quadratic inequality for
\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|-\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)^{-1}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{A}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|\!\left|\!\left|-\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)^{-1}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widehat{\Psi}_{\rho}(x,\widetilde{Q}(x))\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(155)

With Lemma
42
in place, one can then derive from (
155
) that the following quadratic inequality holds uniformly for any
x\in B_{\mu}(L)
under
\widetilde{E}_{2}
.

\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq a_{0}+a_{2}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Here
a_{0},a_{2}>0
are uniform for any
x\in B_{\mu}(L)
and satisfies

\displaystyle a_{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{C}_{\tau,1}^{\prime}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle a_{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{C}_{\tau,2}^{\prime}\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)

for constants
\widetilde{C}_{\tau,1}^{\prime},\widetilde{C}_{\tau,2}^{\prime}>0
independent of
n
.

Therefore, taking the supremum over
x
gives

\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq a_{0}+a_{2}\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Solving the above quadratic inequality for
\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
then gives

\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\in\left[0,\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4a_{0}a_{2}}}{2a_{2}}\right]\cup\left[\frac{1+\sqrt{1-4a_{0}a_{2}}}{2a_{2}},+\infty\right)

Since
\widetilde{E}_{2}\subset\widetilde{E}_{1}
and the slow rate of convergence (Lemma
38
) implies that only the smaller branch should be retained. Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4a_{0}a_{2}}}{2a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4a_{0}a_{2}}{2a_{2}(1+\sqrt{1-4a_{0}a_{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{fast},\tau}\frac{\cdot\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

for some constant
C_{\mathrm{fast},\tau}
independent of
n
.

The proof of Lemma
43
is then complete.

C.9
Proof of Lemma
44

Fix
\tau=10
. Denote

\displaystyle\Delta_{n,\rho}

\displaystyle=\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}\right\},

where
\widetilde{M}_{L}=\left(C_{B}\vee c_{b}\right)^{2}L^{2}L_{\tau}^{4+\left(4\vee 2C_{1}\right)}\geq t_{0}^{2}
is defined in Lemma
36
.
By definition, one has
\widetilde{E}_{2}\subset\widetilde{E}_{0}\subset\widetilde{E}_{\rho}
. Hence one can obtain the following decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{fast},\tau}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}++\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}\right\}

•

Under event
\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}
, one has

\displaystyle\Delta_{n,\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left[\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\vee\left\|Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}

Here (i) follows from Lemma
33
,
36
and the definition of
\widetilde{E}_{\rho}
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n^{\tau-2}}

•

Under event
\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}
, one has

\displaystyle\Delta_{n,\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left[\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\|Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

Lemma
36
implies that under
\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}
, for any
t\geq n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq t\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim h_{0}(\sqrt{t}/n)

\displaystyle\lesssim n\cdot\exp\left[-\left(\frac{\sqrt{t}}{c_{b}Ln}\right)^{2/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}\right]

\displaystyle=n\cdot\exp\left[-\left(\frac{t}{c_{b}^{2}L^{2}n^{2}}\right)^{1/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}\right]

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bm{1}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}}^{\infty}nt\exp\left[-\left(\frac{t}{c_{b}^{2}L^{2}n^{2}}\right)^{1/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}\right]dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{M}_{L}}^{\infty}n^{5}t\exp\left[-\left(\frac{t}{c_{b}^{2}L^{2}}\right)^{1/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}\right]dt

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\int_{\widetilde{M}_{L}}^{\infty}n^{5}t\left\{\exp\left[-\left(\frac{\widetilde{M}_{L}}{c_{b}^{2}L^{2}}\right)^{1/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}-\left(\frac{t}{c_{b}^{2}L^{2}}\right)^{1/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}\right]\right\}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\widetilde{M}_{L}}^{\infty}n^{5}t\left\{n^{-(1+\tau)}\exp\left[-\left(\frac{t}{c_{b}^{2}L^{2}}\right)^{1/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}\right]\right\}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{4-\tau}L^{4}\int_{\widetilde{M}_{L}/(c_{b}L^{2})}^{\infty}t\exp\left[-t^{1/(C_{\Lambda}\vee 2+2)}\right]dt

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{4-\tau}L^{4}

Here (i) follows from the inequality

\displaystyle(a+b)^{\alpha}\geq(a^{\alpha}+b^{\alpha})/2^{1-\alpha},\quad a,b\geq 0,0<\alpha\leq 1

Finally, it follows by combining results above that for
\tau\geq 9/2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Delta_{n,\rho}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

This finishes the proof of Lemma
44
.

C.10
Proof of Lemma
45

Let
\tau>9/2
.
Lemma
21

(iv)
implies that under
\widetilde{E}_{2}
, one also has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}W\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x),Q^{*}(x)\right)\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

The proof then follows similar steps to those in Lemma
44
. Specifically, denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}=\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}W\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x),Q^{*}(x)\right)

Then one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}++\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}\right\}

•

Under event
\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}
, Lemma
21

(i)
implies that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left[\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\vee\left\|Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}

Here (i) follows from Lemma
33
,
36
and the definition of
\widetilde{E}_{\rho}
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}_{2}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n^{\tau-2}}

•

Under event
\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}
, one can similarly obtain that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\bm{1}\left\{\widetilde{E}_{\rho}^{c}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bm{1}\left\{\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>n^{2}\widetilde{M}_{L}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-(\tau-4)}

Finally, one can arrive at the conclusion that for
\tau\geq 9/2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,\rho}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

This finishes the proof of Lemma
