.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
10

To simplify notation, we fix
x
and write
Q^{*}
for
Q^{*}(x)
,
\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}
for
\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}(x)
when there is no ambiguity.

Following the same argument as (
154
) in Appendix
C
, one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(X,Q)\sim\mathbb{P}}\ w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)=0

(156)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right)=\left(-\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}}_{=\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}-I_{d}\right)}_{=:a_{n}(x)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}(x)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x))^{\otimes 2}}_{=:b_{n}(x)}

(157)

Analysis of
\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)
:

Lemma
40
and
41
together imply that for any fixed
x
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)\xlongrightarrow{p}\mathbb{E}\left[-w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(x)}\right]

Then by Assumption
5
, one has

\displaystyle\left[\widehat{\Phi}_{\rho}(x)\right]^{-1}\xlongrightarrow{p}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(-w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}(x)}\right)\right]^{-1}

(158)

Analysis of
b_{n}(x)
:

Theorem
6
, Lemma
40
and
41
together imply that for any fixed
x
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\left|b_{n}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}\cdot\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)\cdot\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(159)

Analysis of
a_{n}(x)
:

by Lemma
26
, one has
w(x,X)=\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\vec{X}
and
w_{n,\rho}(x,X)=\vec{x}^{\top}\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1}\vec{X}
. Hence one can obtain

\displaystyle a_{n}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{x}^{\top}\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1}\vec{X}_{i}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)

(160)

\displaystyle=\left(\vec{x}^{\top}\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{X}_{i}\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{X}_{i}\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)}_{=:a_{n,1}(x)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sqrt{n}\left(\vec{x}^{\top}(\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1}-\vec{\Sigma}^{-1})\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)}_{=:a_{n,2}(x)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sqrt{n}\left(\vec{x}^{\top}(\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1}-\vec{\Sigma}^{-1})\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{X}_{i}\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right]}_{=:a_{n,3}(x)}

•

a_{n,1}(x)
: by (
156
) and Lemma
26
, one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}a_{n,1}(x)=0

(161)

To see this, it suffices to show
\mathbb{E}\left(\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\left(\vec{X}\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})\right)=0
. Direct computation shows that the LHS of the equality is equal to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\vec{X}\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})=0

Here the equality follows from the optimality condition (
156
).

•

a_{n,2}(x)
: using the formula
A^{-1}-B^{-1}=A^{-1}(B-A)B^{-1}
, one can obtain

\displaystyle a_{n,2}(x)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\left(\vec{x}^{\top}\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1}(\vec{\Sigma}-\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1})\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\left(\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}(\vec{\Sigma}-\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho})\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sqrt{n}\left(\vec{x}^{\top}(\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}-\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1})(\vec{\Sigma}-\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}^{-1})\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}(\vec{X}_{i}\vec{X}_{i}^{\top}-\vec{\Sigma})\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d})\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

(162)

Here the last line follows from the fact that
\widehat{\vec{\Sigma}}_{\rho}-\vec{\Sigma}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
which is itself due to the sub-Gaussianity of
X
.

•

a_{n,3}(x)
: note that
\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\vec{X}_{i}\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right]=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
again by the sub-Gaussianity of
X
and Assumption
2
, one can arrive at

\displaystyle a_{n,3}(x)=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})

(163)

Combining (
160
), (
161
), (
162
), (
163
) and the functional central limit theorem
(
Hsing and Eubank,, 2015
, Theorem 7.7.6)
, one can obtain

\displaystyle a_{n}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\left(\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\left(\vec{X}_{i}\otimes(T^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d})\right)-

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\ \left(\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}(\vec{X}_{i}\vec{X}_{i}^{\top}-\vec{\Sigma})\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d})\right)\Big]+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{w}{\to}Z_{x}

(164)

where
Z_{x}\sim{\cal N}\left(0,\Xi_{x}\right)
is a Gaussian random matrix with mean
0
and covariance
\Xi_{x}
. Here
\Xi_{x}:=\mathbb{E}V_{x}\otimes V_{x}
with

\displaystyle V_{x}=w(x,X)\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)-\left(\vec{x}^{\top}\vec{\Sigma}^{-1}(\vec{X}\vec{X}^{\top}-\vec{\Sigma})\otimes I_{d}\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d})\right)

Finally, (
157
), (
158
), (
159
) and (
164
) together imply

\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{Q}_{n,\rho}-Q^{*}\right)\overset{w}{\to}\left(-\mathbb{E}w(x,X)dT^{Q}_{Q^{*}}\right)^{-1}\cdot Z_{x}

which completes the proof.

Appendix E
Proof of Corollary
13

The optimality condition for
Q^{*}(x)
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)=0

Note that independence between
X
and
Q
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\mathbb{E}\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

Here (i) follows from the fact that
\mathbb{E}w(x,X)=1
. Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)=0

By independence, the above equality then implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\vec{X}\otimes(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d})=0

Hence one has
V_{x,2}=0
, the proof is then complete.

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
15

First, we demonstrate in Lemma
46
below uniform fast convergence under Assumption
1
,
2
,
5
,
6
and the null hypothesis. In order to apply Theorem
6
, it suffices to verify Assumption
5
and Assumption
4
. Note that Assumption
6
and the null implies the independence between
X
and
Q
, then both Assumption
5
and
4
are consequences of Lemma
34
.

Lemma 46
.

Instate the assumptions in Theorem
15
. Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L_{n})}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq i\leq n}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

With Lemma
46
in place, we consider the Taylor expansion of the optimality condition for
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
. Recall that the optimality condition for
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)}-I_{d}\right)=0

Then one can apply Lemma
21
to get the following 2nd order Taylor expansion around
Q^{*}(x)
.

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q_{i}}\cdot\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right)+R_{2}(x)

(165)

where
R_{2}(x)
is the 2nd order remainder term with some
\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)
lying between
Q^{*}(x)
and
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)
defined as follows.

\displaystyle R_{2}(x):=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})d^{2}T_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}^{Q_{i}}\cdot\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right)^{\otimes 2}

Under the null hypothesis (
20
), one has
Q^{*}(x)\equiv Q^{*}
and (
165
) reduces to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}\cdot\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right)+R_{2}(x)

(166)

Setting
x=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
in (
166
) then gives

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}\cdot\left(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right)+R_{2}(x)

(167)

Take difference between (
166
) and (
167
) and rearrange, one can obtain

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left(-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}\right)\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))}_{=:\tau(x)}

(168)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-1)(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})}_{=:\alpha_{0}(x)}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-1)dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))}_{=:\alpha_{1}(x)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{R_{2}(x)-R_{2}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}_{=:\alpha_{2}(x)}

Before delving further into the proof, we pause to introduce more notations. Define

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}^{Q_{i}}\right)\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

Then by definition, the test statistic
\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\widehat{\tau}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, and we have the following decomposition of
\tau(x)
.

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}(x)

\displaystyle=\tau(x)+\widehat{\tau}(x)-\tau(x)

\displaystyle=\tau(x)+\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}^{Q_{i}}\right)\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))}_{=:\alpha_{3}(x)}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}(x)+\underbrace{\alpha_{1}(x)+\alpha_{2}(x)+\alpha_{3}(x)}_{=:R(x)}

We also define counterparts of
\alpha_{0},...,\alpha_{2}
and
R_{2}
by replacing
w_{n,\rho}(\cdot,\cdot)
with
w(\cdot,\cdot)
as follows.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w(x,X_{i})-1)(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}(x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w(x,X_{i})-1)dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}(x)

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{R}_{2}(x)-\widetilde{R}_{2}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)

where

\widetilde{R}_{2}(x)=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w(x,X_{i})d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*})^{\otimes 2}

With the above notation in place, one can obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|\alpha_{0}(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\underbrace{2\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\langle\alpha_{0}(X_{k}),R(X_{k})\right\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|R(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{\mathrm{Rem}_{n}}

(169)

The proof is then divided into three steps.

•

First, we give upper bounds for
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}
as well as
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}-\alpha_{i}
and their consequences.

•

Next, we show that the remainder term
\mathrm{Rem}_{n}
is negligible.

•

Then, we demonstrate that
\sum_{i}\left\|\alpha_{0}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
converges weakly to the desired asymptotic null distribution (
26
).

Analysis of
\widetilde{\alpha}
and
\widetilde{\alpha}-\alpha
:

We give uniform upper bounds for
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}(x)-\alpha_{i}(x)
for
i=0,1,2
in Lemma
47
as uniform upper bounds for
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}(x)
and
\alpha_{3}(x)
in Lemma
48
; see Appendix
F.1
,
F.2
for the proof.

Lemma 47
.

Instate the notations and assumptions in Theorem
15
.

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\alpha_{0}(x)-\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}(x)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(170)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\alpha_{1}(x)-\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}(x)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

(171)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\alpha_{2}(x)-\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}(x)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n^{3/2}}

(172)

with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-99}\right)
.

Lemma 48
.

Instate the notations and assumptions in Theorem
15
.

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}(x)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(173)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}(x)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

(174)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}(x)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

(175)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\alpha_{3}(x)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

(176)

with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-99}\right)
.

With the above lemmas in place, one can readily obtain that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-99})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\alpha_{0}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}(X_{i})-\alpha_{0}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(177)

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\alpha_{1}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}(X_{i})-\alpha_{1}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

(178)

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\alpha_{2}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}(X_{i})-\alpha_{2}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

(179)

which then implies

\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|R(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{3}\sup_{i\in[n]}\left\|\alpha_{k}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

(180)

Negligibility of
\mathrm{Rem}_{n}
:

We consider two terms
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\langle\alpha_{0}(X_{k}),R(X_{k})\right\rangle
and
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|R(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
separately.

Analysis of
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\langle\alpha_{0}(X_{k}),R(X_{k})\right\rangle
:
By (
177
) and (
180
), one has with probability at least
1-O(n^{-99})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\langle\alpha_{0}(X_{k}),R(X_{k})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|\alpha_{0}(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\left\|R(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

Analysis of
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|R(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
:
Similarly, by (
180
), one has with probability at least
1-O(n^{-99})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|R(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

Therefore, the above results imply that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-99})
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rem}_{n}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(181)

Analysis of
\alpha_{0}
:

To consider the main term
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\alpha_{0}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|\alpha_{0}(X_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{k}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}),\sum_{j=1}^{n}(X_{k}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{j}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{j}}-I_{d})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{k}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)(X_{k}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{j}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\left\langle T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d},T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{j}}-I_{d}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1}(X_{j}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\left\langle T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d},T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{j}}-I_{d}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Note that

\displaystyle\quad\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})

\displaystyle=\left[(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1/2}\Sigma^{1/2})\otimes I_{d}\right]\cdot\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})\right]

\displaystyle=(1+o_{p}(1))\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})

(182)

Also, one can obtain

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})\right)-\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})\right)

(183)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{-1/2}(\mu-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Sigma^{-1/2}(\mu-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{p}(1)

Here the last line follows since
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu-\mu=o_{p}(1)
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})
is asymptotically normal with zero mean. The zero mean is justified in the following claim whose proof is deferred to Appendix
F.3
.

Claim 4
.

Under the null hypothesis (
20
) and Assumption
4
,
6
,
X
and
Q
are independent and one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X-\mu)\otimes\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=0

(184)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=0

(185)

Claim
4
also implies that
\mathbb{E}\Sigma^{-1/2}(X-\mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})=0
. Therefore, by the functional central limit theorem
(
Hsing and Eubank,, 2015
, Theorem 7.7.6)
, one can obtain

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})\overset{w}{\to}{\cal N}\left(0,I_{p}\otimes\mathbb{E}\left[(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})\right]\right)

(186)

Combining (
182
), (
183
) and (
186
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\rho}^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})\overset{w}{\to}{\cal N}\left(0,I_{p}\otimes\mathbb{E}\left[(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})\otimes(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})\right]\right)

which then implies that

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\|\alpha_{0}(X_{k})\right\|^{2}\overset{w}{\to}\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}w_{i}

(187)

where
w_{i}
are i.i.d.
\chi_{p}^{2}
random variables and
\lambda_{i}
are the eigenvalues of
\mathbb{E}\left[(T_{Q_{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})\otimes(T_{Q_{*}}^{Q}-I_{d})\right]
.

Finally, taking (
169
) (
181
) and (
187
) collectively yields

\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}\overset{w}{\to}\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}w_{i}

F.1
Proof of Lemma
47

Apply Lemma
46
and triangle inequality to see that under the null (
20
), with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(188)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

Proof of (
170
):

note that

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}(x)-\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i})\right)(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-I_{d})

\displaystyle=A_{n}(x)-\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)

Hence (
170
) follows from Lemma
41
.

Proof of (
171
):

one can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\alpha_{1}(x)-\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i}))dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i}))dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left|\!\left|\!\left|\Phi_{n}(x)-\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

Hence (
171
) follows from Lemma
41
and (
188
).

Proof of (
172
):

one can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\alpha_{2}(x)-\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i}))d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*})^{\otimes 2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w_{n,\rho}(x,X_{i})-w(x,X_{i}))d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\!\left|\!\left|\Psi_{n}(x,\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x))-\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x,\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x))\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Hence (
172
) follows from Lemma
41
and (
188
).

F.2
Proof of Lemma
48

Apply Lemma
46
and triangle inequality to see that under the null (
20
), with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(189)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

Proof of (
173
):

Recall the definition of
\widetilde{A}_{n}(x)
in (
79
), one can see that (
173
) follows from (
86
) in Lemma
40
with a slight and straightforward modification to accommodate the
w(x,X)-1
term here. For brevity, we omit the proof.

Proof of (
174
):

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w(x,X_{i})-1)dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(190)

Recall the definition of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(x)
in (
79
), with a slight modification of the proof of (
87
) in Lemma
40
, one can obtain

\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w(x,X_{i})-1)dT^{Q_{i}}_{Q^{*}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(191)

For brevity, the proof is omitted.

Combining (
189
), (
190
) and (
191
) gives (
174
).

Proof of (
175
):

Apply triangle inequality to see that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widetilde{R}_{2}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{R}_{2}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{R}_{2}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

Moreover, one can obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widetilde{R}_{2}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w(x,X_{i})d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}(x)}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{x \in B_\mu(L)}{S \in\cS_d((2M_L)^{-1}, 2M_L)}}\left|\!\left|\!\left|\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(x,S)\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

Here (i) follows from (
88
), (
89
) in Lemma
40
as well as (
189
).

Proof of (
176
):

one can obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\alpha_{3}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}^{Q_{i}}\right)\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}^{Q_{i}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(192)

Moreover, one has

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}dT_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}^{Q_{i}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT_{Q^{*}}^{Q_{i}}-dT_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}^{Q_{i}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{Q_{i,n}^{\prime}}\cdot(\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*})\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{Q_{i,n}^{\prime}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(193)

Here (i) follows from triangle inequality, (ii) from the mean value theorem
(
Dudley and Norvaiša,, 2011
, Theorem 5.3)
for some
Q^{\prime}_{i,n}
that lies on the segment between
Q^{*}
and
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, and (iii) arises from Lemma
34
. Therefore, with a slight modification of the proof of (
89
) in Lemma
40
, one can obtain with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{Q_{i,n}^{\prime}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\leq\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)

(194)

For brevity, the proof is omitted.

Finally, combining (
192
), (
193
) and (
194
) gives

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\alpha_{3}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\!\left|\!\left|d^{2}T^{Q_{i}}_{Q_{i,n}^{\prime}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\right]\cdot\left[\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{n}

which finishes the proof for (
176
).

F.3
Proof of Claim
4

The independence follows directly from Assumption
6
and the null hypothesis (
20
).

Proof of (
184
):

by independence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X-\mu)\otimes\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbb{E}(X-\mu)\right]\otimes\left[\mathbb{E}\left(T_{Q^{*}}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=0

Proof of (
185
):

The optimality condition of
Q^{*}(x)
gives that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)=0

By independence, one then has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\left[\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\right]\cdot\left[\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\left(T_{Q^{*}(x)}^{Q}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=0

Here (i) follows from the fact that
\mathbb{E}w(x,X)\equiv 1
.

Appendix G
Proof of Proposition
17

Note that under the null (
20
), it holds that
Q^{*}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=Q^{*}
. Then Theorem
6
implies that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

Therefore, one has with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\in{\cal S}_{d}((2c_{1})^{-1},2c_{1})

(195)

for
n
large enough. Here we recall that
c_{1}\geq 1
is a constant defined in Assumption
2
.

One can apply the triangle inequality to see that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sup_{S\in{\cal S}_{d}((2c_{1})^{-1},2c_{1})}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{S}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{S}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{\zeta_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sup_{S:\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)/\sqrt{n}}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{\zeta_{2}}

(196)

Here (i) follows from (
195
).

Upper bounds for
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
in (
196
) are summarized in the lemma below whose proof is deferred to Appendix
G.1
. Note that Lemma
49
do not assume the null hypothesis so that it can be reused later for the proof of the power (Theorem
18
).

Lemma 49
.

Suppose Assumption
1
-
6
hold. Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(197)

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(198)

Lemma
49
combined with (
196
) then implies

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(199)

As a result, if
\left(\lambda_{i}\right)_{i\in[d^{2}]}
are sorted in order, then one has
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}\to\lambda_{i}
uniformly for
i\in[d^{2}]
in probability which further implies that

\sum_{i=1}^{d^{2}}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}w_{i}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\sum_{i=1}^{d^{2}}\lambda_{i}w_{i}

Then the continuous mapping theorem implies that
\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}\to q_{1-\alpha}
in probability. Then one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>q_{1-\alpha}-\epsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}-q_{1-\alpha}\right|>\epsilon\right)

Taking the limit as
n\to\infty
, followed by letting
\epsilon\to 0
to get that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}\right)\leq\alpha

(200)

A similar lower bound shows

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widehat{q}_{\alpha}\right)\geq\alpha

(201)

Finally, combining (
200
) and (
201
) completes the proof.

G.1
Proof of Lemma
49

Proof of (
197
):

the proof is similar to Lemma
40
, and is hence omitted for brevity.

Proof of (
198
):

First, recall differential properties (Lemma
21
) that

\displaystyle\left\|T^{Q}_{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(Q\right)\cdot\lambda_{\min}\left(Q\right)^{-1/2}\cdot\lambda_{\min}\left(S\right)^{-1/2}

(202)

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{Q}_{S}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(Q\right)^{1/2}\cdot\lambda_{\min}\left(S\right)^{-2}\cdot\lambda_{\max}\left(S\right)^{1/2}

Also, denote
\phi(Q,S):=\left(T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right)
.

With these results in place, one can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\phi(Q,S)-\phi(Q,Q^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{S}-T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\left(T^{Q}_{S}-T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\left\|T^{Q}_{S}-T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|T^{Q}_{S}-T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\left\|T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{Q}_{S^{\prime}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{Q}_{S^{\prime}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\cdot\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

where
S^{\prime}
lies between
Q^{*}
and
S
. Note that Assumption
2
and the condition
\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)/\sqrt{n}
implies that

\displaystyle S,Q^{*}\in{\cal S}_{d}((2c_{1})^{-1},2c_{1})

Then for any
S\in{\cal S}_{d}((2c_{1})^{-1},2c_{1})
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|T^{Q}_{S}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{Q}_{S^{\prime}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|X-\mu\right\|^{3C_{1}/2}+1\right)\cdot\left\|X-\mu\right\|^{C_{1}/2}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}1

Here
C_{1}
is defined in Assumption
2
, (i) follows from (
202
) and (ii) is a result of the sub-Gaussianity of
X
. Similarly, one has

\mathbb{E}\left\|T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\left|\!\left|\!\left|dT^{Q}_{S^{\prime}}\right|\!\right|\!\right|\lesssim 1

Combining results above, one can obtain

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{S:\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)/\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}_{Q}\left\|\phi(Q,S)-\phi(Q,Q^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{S:\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)/\sqrt{n}}1\cdot\left\|S-Q^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

The proof is then complete.

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
18

As argued at the beginning of Appendix
C.5
, one can assume without loss of generality that
\left\|X-\mu\right\|\leq L
almost surely with
L=C_{L}\sqrt{\log n}
for some constant
C_{L}
large enough as in (
80
). Recall the notation that
M_{L}:=\gamma_{\Lambda}(L)
.

Power under Frobenius norm:

First we demonstrate concentration of various quantities of interest and derive their consequences in Lemma
50
below. The proof is in Appendix
H.1
.

Lemma 50
.

Instate the notations and assumptions in Theorem
18
. Assume in addition that

\left\|X-\mu\right\|\leq L
almost surely.
Then there exists an event
E_{n}
that satisfies
\mathbb{P}(E_{n})\geq 1-O(n^{-100})
for any
\mathbb{P}\in\mathfrak{P}
, under which the following inequalities

\displaystyle\left\|X_{i}-\mu\right\|\leq L,

\displaystyle\quad Q_{i}\in{\cal S}_{d}(M_{L}^{-1},M_{L})\qquad\text{for}\quad i\in[n]

(203)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{\mu}(L)}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x)-Q^{*}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(204)

\displaystyle\left\{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(x):x\in B_{\mu}(L)\right\}

\displaystyle\subset{\cal S}_{d}\left(\left(2^{1/6}M_{L}\right)^{-1},2^{1/6}M_{L}\right)

(205)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{na_{n}^{2}}{2}

(206)

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(207)

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{1},\qquad i\in\left[d^{2}\right]

(208)

hold for
n
large enough.

Next, note that (
(iii)
) in Lemma
21
implies that for any
Q,S\in{\cal S}_{d}\left(\left(2^{1/6}M_{L}\right)^{-1},2^{1/6}M_{L}\right)
(here
2^{1/6}
is chosen only for technical computation), one has

\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}M_{L}^{3}}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(-dT^{Q}_{S}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(-dT^{Q}_{S}\right)\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}M_{L}^{3}

which then implies that under
E_{n}
, the following holds.

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{H}\right)\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\min}\left(-dT^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}M_{L}^{3}}

Therefore, under
E_{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}\geq\frac{1}{8M_{L}^{6}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(X_{i})-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(209)

Then from the following decomposition

\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(X_{i})-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)+\underbrace{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(X_{i})+Q^{*}(\mu)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}_{\Delta_{i}}

one can obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(X_{i})-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\langle Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu),\Delta_{i}\right\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\geq}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(1-2\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right)

(210)

Here (i) follows by developing the square, (ii) is a consequence of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Lemma
50
implies that under
E_{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|Q^{*}(\mu)-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

(211)

Therefore, (
210
) and (
211
) together imply the following inequality for
n
large enough.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(X_{i})-\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\overset{(I)}{\geq}\frac{na_{n}^{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{3}{4}

(212)

Here (I) arises due to Lemma
50
, (
211
) as well as the fact that
\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)=o\left(na_{n}^{2}\right)
.

Combining (
209
) and (
212
) then gives that under
E_{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3na_{n}^{2}}{64M_{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{na_{n}^{2}}

(213)

for
n
large enough.

Denote
\widetilde{q}_{1-\alpha}
the
1-\alpha
quantile of
\sum_{i=1}^{d^{2}}2\lambda_{1}w_{i}
, which is a fixed constant. Then as a consequence of Lemma
50
,
\sum_{i=1}^{d^{2}}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}w_{i}
is stochastically dominated by
\sum_{i=1}^{d^{2}}2\lambda_{1}w_{i}
under
E_{n}
, which then implies that

\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}<\widetilde{q}_{1-\alpha},\qquad\text{under }E_{n}

Therefore, for any
\mathbb{P}\in\mathfrak{P}
, one can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widetilde{q}_{1-\alpha}\right\}\cap\left\{\widetilde{q}_{1-\alpha}>\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widetilde{q}_{1-\alpha}\right\}\cap E_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\geq}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\sqrt{na_{n}^{2}}>\widetilde{q}_{1-\alpha}\right\}\cap E_{n}\right)

for
n
large enough. Here (i) follows from (
213
).

Finally, one can take
n\to\infty
to see that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathfrak{P}}\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathfrak{P}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\sqrt{na_{n}^{2}}>\widetilde{q}_{1-\alpha}\right\}\cap E_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1

which implies

\lim_{n\to\infty}\inf_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathfrak{P}}\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{{\cal T}}_{\rho}>\widehat{q}_{1-\alpha}\right)=1

The proof is then complete.

Power under Wasserstein distance:

by Lemma
21
and the boundedness assumption, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|Q^{*}(X)-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}\mathbb{E}W^{2}\left(Q^{*}(X),Q^{*}(\mu)\right)

Therefore, the 1st part of the proof (Frobenius norm) can be applied.

H.1
Proof of Lemma
50

(
203
)-(
205
) are shown in the proof of Theorem
6
and (
207
) is due to Lemma
32
.

Proof of (
206
):

With these in place, we have almost surely that

\displaystyle\left\|Q^{*}(X)-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq d\left\|Q^{*}(X)-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq dM_{L}^{2}=:M_{F}\asymp\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)

(214)

Define random variable
Y_{i}
as follows.

\displaystyle Y_{i}

\displaystyle:=\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\|Q^{*}(X)-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

With (
214
) in place, one then has
Y_{i}\in[-M_{F},M_{F}]
,
\mathbb{E}Y_{i}=0
and

\displaystyle\Var{Y_{i}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|Y_{i}\right|M_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left\|Q^{*}(X_{i})-Q^{*}(\mu)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot M_{F}

\displaystyle=2a_{n}^{2}\cdot M_{F}

To prove (
206
), it suffices to show that ,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i}^{n}Y_{i}\right|\leq\frac{na_{n}^{2}}{2}\quad\text{with probability at least }1-O(n^{-100})

(215)

To this end, note that by Bernstein’s inequality
(
Vershynin,, 2018
, Theorem 2.8.4)
, one has with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i}^{n}Y_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{F}\log n+\sqrt{n\Var{Y_{i}}}\cdot\sqrt{\log n}

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{F}\log n+\sqrt{na_{n}^{2}M_{F}}\cdot\sqrt{\log n}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)\cdot\left(1+\sqrt{na_{n}^{2}}\right)

(216)

Then (
216
) implies (
215
) for
n
large enough due to the assumption that
na_{n}^{2}\gtrsim n^{2\alpha_{2}}
for some constant
\alpha_{2}>0
. The proof is then complete.

Proof of (
208
):

Note that the proof of Lemma
49
does not assume the null hypothesis. Therefore, with (
207
) in place, one can exactly follow (
195
)-(
199
) as in the proof of Lemma
49
to get

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q_{i}}_{\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}-I_{d}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\otimes\left(T^{Q}_{Q^{*}}-I_{d}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{polylog}\left(n\right)}{\sqrt{n}}

which implies (
208
).

Appendix I
Additional simulations

We conduct simulations to compare different initialization methods and step sizes, as described in Section
5.1
.

I.1
Initialization

To assess the performance of different initialization methods, we conduct simulations based on Example
2
with parameters
n=200
,
p=5
and
d=6
, setting
\eta=1
,
T=30
and
\mathrm{eps}=10^{-6}
in Algorithm
1
. We consider three different initialization methods:

1.

Identity Initialization :
S_{0}=I_{d}
.

2.

Random Initialization:
S_{0}=U\exp(M)U^{\top}
where
U
and
M
are independent;
U\in{\cal O}_{d}
is a random orthogonal matrix following the Haar measure, and
M
is a random diagonal matrix with i.i.d. diagonal entries
M_{kk}\sim{\cal N}(0,1/\sqrt{d})
,
k\in[d]
.

3.

Mean Initialization:
S_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i}
.

Table
1
reports the number of steps before termination and the relative error with respect to the identity initialization, averaged over 100 simulations. In each simulation, after generating
n=200
pairs of
(X_{i},Q_{i})
, we randomly generate
\widetilde{X}\sim\mathrm{Uniform}[-1,1]^{p}
and compute
\widehat{Q}_{\rho}(\widetilde{X})
using Algorithm
1
with the three initialization methods described above.

Table 1
:
Comparison of different initialization methods.

\delta

Initialization method

Relative error (
\times\mathrm{eps}
)

It can be observed that all three methods converge to the same point within nearly the same number of steps, as the relative error is significantly smaller than the threshold
\mathrm{eps}
.
